# erd-editor

## Purpose

The editor core, published on npm: the framework-free `<erd-editor>` custom element on `@dineug/r-html`, with a Redux-like store whose actions carry a Lamport clock version and merge through the LWW registers in `@dineug/erd-editor-schema`. `app`, `webview-client`, `replication-store-worker`, `obsidian-plugin`, which bundles it into its `main.js`, and `mcp-server` depend on it. `app` imports the DOM-free `engine.js` entry beside the element and `replication-store-worker` imports that entry alone, and `mcp-server` inlines the DOM-free `peer.js` entry into its one file, a headless collaboration peer that edits by dispatching actions instead of by a pointer; `app`'s Drive and fingerprint specs import that entry too.

The ERD scene is a Konva `<canvas>` rendered through a second r-html host (`src/konva/`); toolbar, panels, menus and the editing overlay stay DOM. The same scene components also draw the one **view**, the Visualization tab's Flow mode — the reader's own placement, zoom and rows, never in the file, the history or a peer — told which through a `GeometrySource`.

Known limit: a replica holds only the registers of what reaches it, so one that no `mergeLWW` answer reaches can apply a setter older than one its element refused, until the element answers a later join and that answer reaches its replica too: `obsidian-plugin`'s, started anew on each load while the element keeps its registers, which no tab is asked for when an outside change of the file reloads into the open shared store, and which the other tabs' answers to a new one need not all hold; and `app`'s per-tab fallback, whose element alone takes the other tabs' answers, as it alone takes their batches (`packages/app/AGENTS.md`) |

## For AI Agents

### Working In This Directory

**Engine**

- **Adding an action** touches the module's `actions.ts`, `atom.actions.ts` and `history.ts` (whose undo entry puts it in `HistoryActionTypes`) **and** the hand-kept lists in `src/engine/actions.ts`. Missing `ChangeActionTypes` compiles, then silently breaks the host `change` (autosave), collaboration and readonly. Readonly is enforced only there (`readonlyIgnoreFilter`, `hasReadonlyIgnore`), never in components. A new change type also needs a tool or an exclusion reason in `mcp-server`'s reachability spec.
- Reducers write through the LWW operators from `@dineug/erd-editor-schema` with `action.version`; a direct write wins every merge and corrupts collaborative sessions.
- **The Schema SQL scripts are document content** (`settings.ddlScripts`, owner decisions of 2026-10-07): one `before` and one `after` string for the whole document, written as is into the DDL of every database. `settings.changeDDLScript` writes each through a register of its own (`replaceOperator`, `settings.ddlScripts` with the position as the path), so a peer's edit of one script never overwrites the other, ignores a position or a value no editor sends, makes an undo entry (`settings/history.ts`) and is dropped under readonly like every change; `mcp-server`'s `erd_set_ddl_script` sends it too. How the DDL is generated (create, IF NOT EXISTS or drop and re-create) and its header are no setting at all, so no action, file or peer carries them (Schema SQL tab, below). An editor built before the scripts ignores the action, and saving a document there drops them.
- **A scroll or zoom is still a change**, unlike one in a Flow view (View seams, below). Scroll and zoom (`settings.scrollTo`, `streamScrollTo`, `changeZoomLevel`, `streamZoomLevel`) stay in `ChangeActionTypes`, which following and shared views need, and the replica reports each as a `change`: every hub (`agent-hub-host`'s `joinWindow.ts`, its Kotlin port) waits for one replica save per change before `join` or `save` answers, and a missing one holds them to their caps and has `save` answer `saved: false`. A zoom moves the origin too, keeping the point it zooms around in place (`changeZoomLevelAction$`, `streamZoomLevelAction$`, `pinchZoomAction$`).
- **Locks** (owner decisions of 2026-10-06): `settings.lockSettings` (`LockSettingType`) locks the viewport, the origin and the zoom as one since one saved without the other opens elsewhere, the tab, the language, both name cases and the bracket type, every one on in a new document. `toJson` writes a locked setting at `settings.lockedValues`, so a change to it comes out `changed` false and the hosts write nothing and mark nothing modified (`vscode-extension`'s `erd-editor.ts`, `obsidian-plugin`'s `ErdView.ts`, `intellij-plugin`'s `ErdEditor.kt`); `app`'s Drive save leaves a view no lock holds out of its fingerprint. The element's `change` event leaves such a change out (`lockIgnoreFilter` on `rx-store.ts`'s `change$`, by `LockSettingActionTypes`, the lock read as the batch passes), while the replica still reports it for the hubs. `changeLockSettingsAction$` locks at what the store holds and carries the values, so every peer and the replica hold one lock, and unlocks carrying what the store shows of the code settings it names (`CodeLockSettings`: the language, both name cases and the bracket type, whose setters every peer applies), which every peer and replica then show, so all save one value although a reader that opened the file while the lock was on shows the value it held (`replication-store.test.ts`, an unlock after a reader joined from the file); an unlock of the viewport or the tab carries nothing, each reader's screen its own; taken on the Settings tab, the tab locked is `editor.lastCanvasType`, the last tab other than Settings the reader stood on, which `changeCanvasType` and both load reducers record before and after they move it. The reducer writes each bit through `replaceOperator` (`settings.lockSettings`, the bit as the path), so the latest lock or unlock of a setting wins on every peer, and refuses a lock whose values the setting's own setter would refuse, the Settings tab included; an unlock sets what it carries whether it wins the bit or not, skipping a value the setter would refuse. The four code setters and an unlock's values write through one register per setting (`replaceCodeSetting`, `settings.code`, the field as the path), outside the bit's, so a setter and an unlock that cross land one value on every peer, and an unlock and a newer relock that cross show one value on every screen while the relock holds the file (`replication-store.test.ts`, an unlock crossing a newer relock), a version tie excepted as everywhere. Locking a setting is a change of its own, readonly-ignored like the rest. `settings.changeIgnoreSaveSettings`, the switch the locks replaced, has a reducer that changes nothing and is in the replica's lists alone (`ReplicaChangeActionTypes`, which `ReplicaActionTypes` takes in), not `ChangeActionTypes`: the replica reports a batch an agent written before the locks sends (`changed` false), so the hub waiting on it confirms the save, while the element fires no `change` and no peer is sent it. The Settings tab's Lock rows (`components/settings/lockSettingRows.ts`) show the value each lock holds, or dimmed the live one while it is off, by the name the menu that sets it gives, the viewport as its zoom and its origin shortened with the unit the content compass prints (`formatDistance`: `12k`, `-2.4k`).
- **A new document starts with every lock on.** The store's initial state is `erd-editor-schema`'s `createSchema()` (`lockSettings` 63), which is what an element given no value, a replica's or a peer's `value` before a load (`app`'s new Drive file, `mcp-server`'s `createEmptyDocument`) and every empty value a load takes show: `utils/loadValue.ts`'s `toLoadValue` turns an empty text into that document's JSON for `setInitialValue`, the `value` setter, `setDiffValue`, the replica and the peer, so the replicated `editor.loadJson` carries text any peer, a released one included, parses to the same document. A file keeps the locks it names, and one that names none (an older v3 file, a v2 `.vuerd`) opens with all of them on, its origin, zoom and tab where a new document's are (`erd-editor-schema`'s `resetPreLockView`). A SQL, DBML, AML or GraphQL import keeps every setting of the document it replaces but the view, the locks included and the view locked where the parser left it (`withImportSettings`, under `toSchemaImportJson`, which `loadSchema*Action$` and a placed import share). A source given to an element with no value, as `app`'s `convertSource` does, so becomes a new document too, and one imported into a file keeps that file's locks (`useErdEditorAttachElement.test.ts`). `clear()` keeps every setting, so a cleared file keeps its locks. Text the parser cannot read, a file with merge-conflict markers, empties the document and changes no setting (r-html logs the reducer's throw), so a store that loaded nothing before shows a new document (`replication-store.test.ts`). Time Travel's preview opens on the reader's scroll and zoom whatever the lock says (`toPreviewJson`), since it is the reader's own screen; every load that replaces the open document (`editor.loadJson`: an import, the undo of a load or a clear, a peer's load, the `value` setter) keeps the screen of each locked setting but the viewport where it was on each store (`landLoadedSettings`, the owner's call on 2026-10-06 for imports), taking each lock through its register and a bit no lock owns from the load, as `show`, while the view lands where the load puts it and is pulled as any load's is; a load that opens a document (`editor.initialLoadJson`) takes everything the file holds. A load from a release before the locks names none, so it turns every lock on, as opening a file it wrote would.
- **`changed` compares values, never the file.** The replica keeps the value its last `change` handed out, or after a load takes the value the first change action finds, which holds the load's own rewrites: `setInitialValue` collects the schema GC's tombstones and runs `settleLoad` (`store-hooks.ts`) before it returns, writing at once what the hooks a load wakes write over the next 5 ms, every `ui.width*` measured again with this machine's fonts and the relationship anchors (`recalculateTableWidthHook`, 5 ms), the foreign key bits and each relationship's `identification` and `startRelationshipType` (the microtasks after the load), a new relationship's values for one removed or whose end columns are all gone, so a pan replayed right behind the load is no edit. A new hook on any action a load dispatches, its `editor.clear` or `editor.initialClear` and the `editor.validationIds` a replica's GC dispatches after it included, joins `settleLoad`, as `store-hooks.test.ts` holds by recording what both load generators and a replica's `setInitialValue` reduce, and joins the fields `app`'s `toFingerprint` and `toDriveFingerprint` leave out (`utils/documentFingerprint.ts`, with a case in `service.test.ts`; `packages/app/AGENTS.md`), since `app` also fingerprints a converted source and another tab's Drive base, which carry no `settleLoad`, and would count the hook's write as an edit. A file an older release or another machine wrote, a v2 `.vuerd`, a byte order mark or a hand edit all differ from that value from the load on, so a host that compared bytes with its file would take the first scroll for an edit. A load that lands while a change is pending reports that change with `changed` false. `engine/replication-store.test.ts` pins a new document and a new empty file (every lock on), the viewport locked and unlocked, a locked view held at its lock wherever the screen goes, a zoom saved with the origin it moved once unlocked, a tab switch and a language locked and unlocked, a batch of the switch the locks replaced, a file saved before the locks, another machine's widths, a file without the origin, a relationship left with no end column, a scroll or zoom before, between and after the hooks a stale file's load wakes and the registers a join brings in before the first change.
- **The foreign key bits and each relationship's `identification` and `startRelationshipType` are read whole off the document** (`validateForeignKeys` in `table-column/hooks.ts`, `recalculateIdentification` and `recalculateStartRelationshipType` in `relationship/hooks.ts`), so peers that took the same actions in another order hold the same values. A column's bit is on exactly while its table is in the document, lists it, and a relationship in the document ends on it there, so a removed column or one of a removed table, which the file holds until the next load, has it off whatever order its peers heard the removal and the link in. A relationship reads only the end columns its end table holds while that table is in the document, and with none left it is not identifying and dashed, as a new one starts; a removed one reads none, so the tombstone the file holds carries those values on every peer, whatever it was remapped or keyed onto before the removal arrived. The bits are read once per batch, in the microtask after it, on a relationship added, removed or remapped (`relationship.changeColumns`), a column or table added or removed (an undo's included) and a load: no timer, since `mcp-server`'s headless session writes its file one scheduler turn after a batch. The two relationship fields are read the same way on those actions and the primary key flag, and the start on the not null flag too, which a key turned on sets with no action of its own, in the microtask after its batch: so the start is read one microtask after the identification, or a batch keying two end columns at once left it ringed (`peer-store.node.test.ts`). Bits set and cleared one relationship at a time cleared a column another relationship still ended on, and a link that woke no hook left the fields at their defaults on one peer and derived on another.
- Reader-only state (`editor.views`, `visualizationMode`, `handTool`, `zenMode`, `openMap`, `lastCanvasType`) is in no list, so it is never saved, undone or sent to a peer; `engine/actions.test.ts` pins the ten `editor.view*` types and `editor.changeVisualizationMode` out of all of them.
- **View seams** (`rx-store.ts`): while `getActiveView` finds one, `viewActionRedirect` rewrites `settings.scrollTo` / `streamScrollTo` / `changeZoomLevel` / `streamZoomLevel` into their `editor.view*` twin (not `Tag.shared` / `Tag.following`), and `viewIgnoreFilter` drops `ViewIgnoreActionTypes` (readonly's list minus `editor.loadJson` / `editor.clear`), letting `Tag.shared` reach the store. `undo` / `redo` no-op while a view is active.
- **Both seams classify a batch by the state it enters with**, so leaving a view and moving the document are two dispatches — `goToErdTable` (`erd/canvas/table/goToErd.ts`) is the model; `e2e/specs/visualization-flow.spec.ts` asserts what the host hears.
- **The foreign key columns a relationship creates are named in one place**, `toForeignKeyNames` (`engine/modules/relationship/fkColumns.ts`), which the pointer draw (`selectTableAction$`), `addRelationshipAction$` and a New column row of Map Columns (`mapColumnsAction$`, through `toForeignKeyActions`) all run: a key of one word (`id`, `ID`, `uuid`, `id2`; `isSingleWord`) gets the parent table and an underscore in front, a key of several words (`member_id`, `userId`, `UserID`) or equal to the table name keeps its name, and a name the child already holds is numbered `_2`, `_3`. Only an equal name escapes the prefix: a key of one word that holds the table name gets it too (`user.userid` gives `user_userid`, `회원.회원번호` gives `회원_회원번호`), as the owner decided. Any character but a letter, mark or digit splits words, so `_id` and `order-no` keep theirs, and words split where camelCase splits them, so `IDs` and `UUIDv4` do too while `Ids` is one word, as is a name in one case (`memberid`, `USERID`: `members_memberid`), and where a letter without case (`\p{Lo}` or `\p{Lm}`: Hangul, kana with its `ー`, Han) meets a cased one, either way, so `회원ID`, `ユーザーID`, `用户ID` and `IDカード` keep theirs while `회원번호` is one word; digits and marks are passed over when reading case, so `SHA256sum` is several words and `md5sum` one. The table name is used as written, dots and spaces included (`public.users` gives `public.users_id`), as the owner decided: such a column makes valid DDL only under a quoting `bracketType`, where every generator also keeps the whole name in the relationship's constraint (`"FK_public.users_TO_orders"`). Unquoted, SQL Server, Oracle, MySQL, MariaDB and PostgreSQL give that constraint the table part alone (`FK_users_TO_orders`, Code generators below), SQLite writes it unnamed, and Snowflake and Databricks keep the whole name (`FK_public.users_TO_orders`). `fkColumns.test.ts` pins each case. Relating existing columns (Map Columns, `erd_link_columns`, the importers, duplicates) renames nothing; Map Columns alone changes their type, bringing each child column it maps to its parent's type through the data type sync in the same batch, and only while `settings.relationshipDataTypeSync` is on. A copy of a serial key takes the integer type it stores, whatever the data type sync, by `toReferenceDataType` (`relationship/referenceType.ts`, DOM-free for the peer): PostgreSQL's own `smallserial`, `bigserial` and `serial2` / `4` / `8` by PostgreSQL's types under any database, a bare `serial` by `settings.database` (`integer` in PostgreSQL, `bigint unsigned` in MySQL and MariaDB, kept elsewhere), a whole name only, in capitals when the key's is, and no saved document migrated (owner decisions); that sync (`table-column/utils/dataType.ts`) maps a key's change into its foreign keys the same way and carries a foreign key's change back to its key unless the key's type is serial, which keeps it and stops the change there. `mcp-server`'s `erd_add_relationship` and `erd_change_column_data_type` descriptions (`tools/copy.ts`) state these rules to agents, so they change with the code.
- Keep host integrations on `ErdEditorElement`. `webview-client`'s `mountWebview` sets the import callback only when a host passes `importFile` (VSCode does, IntelliJ does not), so the built-in `<input type=file>` is IntelliJ's only import path.
- **An import is placed before it lands** (`utils/file/importSchema.ts`, owner decisions of 2026-10-04): one picked from the menu, the palette or a host's file dialog (`webview-client` passes `placement: 'auto'`), and a `setSchema*` call only when given `{ placement: 'auto' }` (as `app`'s `convertSource` does on a detached element, waiting for the Promise before it reads `value`), parses once (`toSchemaImportJson`), measures that document on a store of its own (`automatic-table-placement/flowPlacement.ts`, as the PNG export draws on its own), lays it out by Flow on the ELK worker through `layoutByElk` (`elkPlacement.tsx`, the core `runElkPlacement` shares, which takes the app context and nothing of `Erd`, and whose Cancel and stop key take the toast down at once, Auto Layout's included) and replaces the document with one `loadJsonAction$` carrying the points from `TABLE_SORT_START`, so one undo puts the previous document back and no grid shows in between. Fewer than two tables or no relationship, a failure, no `SharedWorker` and Cancel on the toast a slow layout raises (`SLOW_LAYOUT_MS`; the stop key cancels only while it is up) land the grid without a word. A load meanwhile, from anyone, supersedes it, and so does a placed replace started after it, so the replace started last lands (an append supersedes nothing, below); the settings it carries are read again as it lands, so a tab or setting changed while it placed stays. A readonly editor places nothing by any path, the menu's and the palette's included: `importSchemaPlaced` reads `store.getReadonly()` before it parses, since the readonly filter drops the load only once the layout, and the toast a slow one raises, have run. `placeByFlow` answers points from the corner of the block the tables make, so a caller placing a block elsewhere moves it. `mcp-server`'s imports keep the grid: elkjs is outside the peer graph's allowlist and its bundle budget.
- **Import and Add appends instead of replacing** (owner decisions of 2026-10-04): the menu beside Import, its five palette commands, a host's import carrying `mode: 'append'` (the editor names a mode only for an append, so a host that knows none replaces; `webview-client` hands it to the setter) and a `setSchema*` or `setSchemaJSON` call given `{ mode: 'append' }` add the file as new entities, the way a paste makes them: `toSchemaAppend` (`editor/generator.actions.ts`) reads the document through `toDuplicateInput` and `toCreateEntityActions` with `valuesOnly`, which sends no field left at what a new entity starts with, so every table gets a new id, a table of a name already there stays beside it, the relationships and indexes ride only between the tables the file brings (the converters link nothing outside it, so a foreign key to an existing table is dropped), and no setting changes. The block stands `APPEND_GAP` below `getContentRect` of the document at dispatch time, united with the boxes of its table groups while they are hidden (which still hold their tables, an owner decision of 2026-10-10), in line with its left edge (`TABLE_SORT_START` in an empty one), stacked over it: a json file keeps its own placement, SQL, GraphQL, DBML and AML take Flow from `placeByFlow` behind the same rules as a placed replace (`appendSchemaPlaced`, `utils/file/importSchema.ts`) but supersede nothing, since placed imports land in the order they started (`placeAndLand`): an append waits for those before it, lands on the document a replace among them brings, and only a load or a placed replace started after it drops it; else the grid `sortTable` lays a replace out in, run on the document's own copy. The editor lands it in one dispatch with the new tables, memos and table groups alone selected (a group beside its members, so a drag of the block carries it and its drop judges none of them, where an unselected group would lose them all once the block left its stored rect) and `scrollIntoView` on their box, clear of an open Find and Replace panel (`coveredWidth`, as `goToErdTarget` lands), so one undo takes back both, after a dispatch of its own that takes the ERD tab from any other (`showErdTab`: the owner decision of 2026-10-04 names Graph mode, Schema SQL and Code Generator; Settings follows it as another tab off the canvas, and a Flow view must, since `viewIgnoreFilter` would drop every add); text the parser cannot read, or a document with nothing in it, adds nothing and switches nothing. `appendSchemaAction$` and `appendSchemaJsonAction$` are the engine generators, in the editor barrel, which `mcp-server`'s imports run for `mode: 'append'`: the grid, no selection, no scroll. A readonly editor offers no Import and Add, in the menu or the palette (owner decision of 2026-10-04), and adds nothing by any path: `landAppend` reads `store.getReadonly()` first, since the readonly filter drops the adds but not the selection, the scroll or the tab.
- **Table groups** (`engine/modules/table-group/`; the DOM-free helpers in `utils/tableGroup.ts`, inside the peer graph): membership, an owner decision, is the table's `groupId`, written through one register per table (`table.changeGroup`, path `groupId`), so a table is in one group at most and two peers placing it converge on the later write; a `groupId` naming no group in `doc.tableGroupIds` reads as none everywhere (`getTableGroupId`, `getTableGroupMemberIds`). A group stores its own rect, and its box is that rect united with each member's rect grown by `TABLE_GROUP_PADDING`, and on top by `TABLE_GROUP_TITLE_HEIGHT` more (`padRect`, `getTableGroupRect`, whose `excludeTableIds` leaves out the tables a drag holds and whose `members` spares the walk over every table), so a member that grows sticks out of nothing and writes nothing; a membership test reads a table's centre in the topmost box (`findTableGroupAt`: the highest zIndex, of two at one the later in `doc.tableGroupIds`; `findTableGroupsAt` answers many points off one read of the boxes, as a drop does, and builds none for no point). Whatever needs every group reads them out of one walk over the tables, never a walk per group: `getTableGroupRects` for every box (`seedTableGroupBoxes` / `growTableGroupBox`, which the content rect folds into its own pass), `getTableGroupMembers` for every member list, since a pan step reads the content rect several times over and a drag step reads the drop boxes. `getTableGroupDrops` judges the selected tables no selected group carries, reads no box with none to judge (a drag of groups alone), and hands each drop the box of the group it lands in, a group the drag leaves standing reaching over the box it held as the drag began (`HeldTableGroupBoxes`, Konva scene, below). `removeTableGroupAction$` clears every member's `groupId` and removes the group in one dispatch, so one undo brings back both, and removing a table needs nothing more. Moving a group (`moveTableGroupAction$`, and `moveAllAction$` over a selected one, `toMoveTableGroupActions`) moves its members by the same relative step in the same dispatch, a member selected beside it once, tagged as a drag; `tableGroup.move` and `tableGroup.changeColor` regroup with the table and memo streams, so a drag is one undo entry. A drag carries the tables its first step read (`getCarriedTableIds`, kept in the `MoveAllGesture` that `useMoveEntity` makes per press and hands every step), since the history and the outbound compressor sum a burst's steps per table list and drop a list under 20 px: a member a peer adds or takes out midway would otherwise leave a step applied here and never sent or undone. `SelectType.tableGroup` comes from a press (`selectTableGroupAction$`, raising the group among groups alone by `nextTableGroupZIndex`, `tableGroup.changeZIndex` in no list, as `memo.changeZIndex`), from adding a group, which selects it alone, and from an append's landing: select all and the marquee never take a group, while Delete (`removeSelectedAction$`, and `getRemovableColumns` reading a selected group as a selection beside the table) and the color picker reach it. A paste or a duplicate carries no `groupId`; an append carries its document's groups under new ids, its tables put in them by their new ids (`toAppendTableGroups`): a file's rects move with the block, which starts at their corners too, and a grid or placed layout wraps each group with members round where they land, the block starting at the wrapped corners as well (`toLayoutGroupRects`). `tableGroup.resize` is one undo entry per write, undone to the rect the group stored before it, which a sash drag writes once, at its end (Konva scene, below). A drop's `table.changeGroup` carries the `groupId` it replaces as `prevValue` and `Tag.drag`, which the `@@move` regroup in `rx-store.ts` takes in by tag (`groupByStreamActions`), so its undo closes into the drag's entry; the undo builder reads `prevValue`, since that entry is built once the reducer has run. `addTableGroupAction$` and `addTableGroupFromTablesAction$` take an optional name and color, which `mcp-server`'s `erd_add_table_group` sets in the batch that adds the group, where the editor names a group by its name editor afterwards. `peer.js` exports both group barrels for `mcp-server`'s group tools, which send every group change type but `tableGroup.moveTo`, which no gesture sends either.
- **Automatic placement keeps table groups together** (an owner decision; `services/elk-layout/`, `automatic-table-placement/`, the grid `sortTable`), whether the groups are shown or hidden, since membership is data and the Show bit only draws. Every ELK placement but the views' preset (`keepsTableGroups`: a view draws no group) sends each group's members inside a node of the group (`kind: 'tableGroup'`, `elk.padding` the box the editor draws, the placement's own options repeated on it, since inside a compound ELK falls back to its default spacing), and each set of groups and tables that relationships join across a group border inside a `component` node carrying `elk.hierarchyHandling: INCLUDE_CHILDREN`, which is what makes ELK read an edge between two groups. The root never carries it: there it switches off `elk.separateConnectedComponents` and stacks every table nothing joins in the first layer (measured 2026-10-10 with elkjs 0.12.0), which the packing case of `elkLayoutService.test.ts` guards. `toElkGraph` hands each edge to the deepest node holding both ends (`containerOf`), so a document with no group gets the flat graph it always did, and `toTablePoints` / `toViewPoints` count each group's padded box in the bounds, so a placed import's group starts at `TABLE_SORT_START`. The force simulation moves a group with members as one box, its members kept where they stand inside it, drawn where it goes in the preview, and holds that box `GROUP_BOX_GAP` clear of every other group's box and table (`forceGroupBoxes`), since a circle sized for a table lets a group several members wide run into a neighbour, where a member's centre would join it to the other group on its next drag; a group's `forceCollide` circle is the one inside its box, so it claims no room past it, while a table keeps its circle, so a document with no group lands where it always did; `sortTable` lays a group's members in rows of their own inside one cell where its first member comes, wrapped so the box fits the canvas width, and writes the group's rect. Whatever placed them, each group with members lands at its members' padded bounds (`getTableGroupWraps`, DOM-free, in the peer graph), in the dispatch that moves the tables: `toPlacementActions` for Auto Layout and the preview's Apply, with the scroll, so one undo; `toLandingJson` for a placed replace; `toSchemaAppend` for an append; the reducer itself for a grid. A group with no member keeps its rect, and no placement changes membership.

**Scene sources and views**

- **`GeometrySource` picks coordinates, shown set and sort channel at once.** Pure functions take a trailing `source = 'document'`; scene components read `useSceneSource(ctx)`. Each scene root (`Erd`, `ErdViewer`, `AutomaticTablePlacement`, `TimeTravel`, and `FlowScene` in `VisualizationFlow.tsx`) provides it **inside a wrapper, never on the root it returns**: `useProvider` attaches to the parent element, so siblings would inherit it.
- Outside any provider, code that must follow the reader (zoom generators via `getActiveTransform`, the view reducers) reads `getActiveView`.
- **A scene writes to the slot it reads**: `sceneScrollToAction(source, …)`, `changeZoomLevelAction$(value, source)`, `moveAllAction$(dx, dy, source)`, `kind` on a view payload. An unnamed scroll or zoom lands on the active view; an unnamed `moveAllAction$` goes to the document, and an open view drops it.
- **`viewLayout.ts` decides what a view shows**: only the tables in `positions` (nothing until a layout lands), **no memos**, relationships with both ends shown, rows per `showMode`; `settings.show` and `columnOrder` do not apply. Hover and pin are module observables keyed on `editor.id`, never actions.
- **`flowLayout.tsx` is the one place a Flow placement is asked for** (`TablePlacement.viewLayered`). Landings are cached under `placedKey` — centers, show mode, the doc's id lists, **never `positions`**, or the loop re-asks on its own answer. Narrow or widen only through `components/flowCenters.ts` or `focusFlowTableAction$`.
- `toHints` (`services/elk-layout/elkGraph.ts`) normalizes coordinate hints; handed raw coordinates, ELK's INTERACTIVE layering reads one document row as one layer per table.

**Relationship geometry**

- `draw-relationship/sort.ts` is the whole geometry pass, routing (`route.ts`, `nudge.ts`) included because it needs every table and route; `pathFinding.ts` only draws.
- **A rendering-only value goes in the `WeakMap` sort channels of `draw-relationship/index.ts`, never a new schema field** — anchors are schema fields and serialize. One channel per source; a view keeps its anchors there, so `setAnchors` writes the entity only for the document. `clearSortChannel` clears per connector, since channels are module-wide.
- View channel setters bump `viewVersions` **only on an actual change**; an unconditional bump redraws every static connector on each drag sort.
- **A connector shows its referential actions at its child end** (`relationship/RelationshipActionLabel.tsx`, an owner decision of 2026-10-03), after erwin's notation: `D:` for ON DELETE, then `U:` for ON UPDATE, each only when set, with `C`, `R`, `SN`, `SD`, `NA` (`utils/referentialAction.ts`), in the code face and the route's colour, lit with it on hover (the group's `hovered`, which the labels alone read, and `hoverRelationshipMap`), on a box of the canvas colour that clears the widest marker: over a route leaving sideways, reaching away from the table, and right of one leaving up or down. `RelationshipGroup` draws every label after every route, so a connector crossing one runs under its box; drawn inside each connector's own group, a label lay under every connector drawn after it. The box is sized from the code face's advance (`0.61` of the size, as the alternate key marks are), so the text is never measured. It is the document's notation alone, a view drawing no distinction by kind, and a zoom that draws a table by its name alone (`isHighLevelTable`) draws none. A hover spells the clauses out in the canvas's native tooltip, the `title` the stage container takes while the pointer is on the connector (`Relationship.tsx`), as the toolbar's buttons name themselves, written again on each render so a peer's change under a resting pointer shows; it shows with the label hidden too, and leaves with the pointer or the connector. View Option's Referential Actions hides the label: it sets `Show.hideReferentialAction`, one of the two bits that hide what they name (`hideTableGroup` the other), so every document, those saved before it included, shows the labels until a reader turns them off. Showing or hiding them moves nothing, so the sort hook skips that toggle (`isPaintOnlyToggle`, which skips the group bit too) and the table size key masks the bit out.
- **Connector ends are compact and corners round** (owner decisions of 2026-10-10, issue #468): the cardinality markers are 85% of their first size (`LINE_SIZE` 6, `LINE_HEIGHT` 9, `CIRCLE_RADIUS` 5, the guide line from 21px), the size Workbench and Liam ERD draw them at, and a side's stubs run 24/32/40/48 with a floor of 24 (`draw-relationship/index.ts`), so two facing tables about 50px apart, which narrow tables shown by name alone sit at, join in one straight run. Every connector draws its markers; an option to hide them was left out. A document corner turns on a quarter circle of `ROUTE_CORNER_RADIUS`, flattened to `ROUTE_CORNER_SEGMENTS` chords (`corner.ts`) as a view's curve is (`bezier.ts`), so the hit path, the route box and the bench still read straight segments; the router and the nudge pass keep working on the orthogonal polyline. A self loop is one curve round the table's top-right corner (`loop.ts`): its anchors sit 20px from the corner on every table and 18px further for each further loop, closer together where the shorter side has no room (`placeLoops`), and its stubs and control points scale with that offset, so a table's loops are one shape at nested sizes that never cross or touch the table; the route box reaches its curve (`loopReach`). A child end's referential action label can hide up to 14px of a run that turns into the end from the label's side, now that the stub ends inside the label's width; the owner accepted that over moving the label or lengthening those stubs (2026-10-10), and the same for a loop's label, which can cover its own curve and, on a table with several loops, the end markers of the loop inside it.
- The sort hooks (`engine/modules/relationship/hooks.ts`) open a 5 ms window for a `Tag.drag` action and sort in a microtask otherwise; a timer loses to the next frame, which draws moved tables with stale connectors.

**Keys and alternate keys**

- **A key the columns declare is never an index entity.** `utils/tableKeys.ts` derives, at render time, the primary key and each unique column (`getColumnKeys`, the read-only rows the Indexes tab lists above a separator, each with a lock where an index has its remove button) and the alternate keys: every unique index over two or more of the table's columns (`getAlternateKeys`), whose member columns carry `AK<n>.<m>` by their place in `indexColumnIds` (`getAlternateKeyMarks`). A single-column unique index and a UQ flag, the same constraint in two spellings a Snowflake round trip swaps, carry no number. Nothing is stored but the `Show.columnAlternateKey` bit, off in every existing document, so no diagram widens until a reader shows the marks.
- **AK numbers follow the table's column order, never `doc.indexIds`**: keys compare by the positions of their columns in key order, a prefix first, the index id last. An undo re-adds an index at the end of `doc.indexIds` and peers append concurrent adds in arrival order, so numbering by that list swapped numbers on undo and differed between collaborators (`tableKeys.test.ts` pins both); reordering the columns may renumber instead.
- **A mark always stands last in its row, in no column order.** It is derived and never edited, so it is no `ColumnType`, no `FocusType` and no entry of `settings.columnOrder`: `getAlternateKeyMarkX` puts it one margin past the last cell `getColumnCellSlots` lays out, whatever the order, and the cells keep `getColumnCellsX`, so the column names stay in line with the header name and the edit overlay and the drag ghost never move for it. Its `k-text` does not listen, so a press on it is a press on the row, as between two cells; keyboard focus, the cell editor, copy and paste walk the order and never reach it. Graph mode's DOM card (`visualization/table/`) draws it last too.
- **The marks widen only the tables that carry one.** `calcAlternateKeyWidth` sizes the room after the last cell from the longest mark at `COLUMN_ALTERNATE_KEY_CHAR_WIDTH` per character (the code face at the cell size, no text measure, so the peer sizes it too), kept in `ColumnWidth.alternateKey` and added to the row sum like any other cell. The seven actions that add, drop, reshape or renumber a key (the five index ones, `moveIndexColumnAction`, and `moveColumnAction`, which renumbers) are in the sort hook's `layoutActions`, which bumps the width generation the size cache keys on, but the hook lets them through only while the bit is set, and they are in `isDocumentOnly`, since a view's geometry sees none of them.
- **A table reads only its own indexes.** `getTableIndexIds` groups `doc.indexIds` by table once per add or drop and caches the grouping on the list, so a sort measures every table in one pass over the indexes, not one per table. The two index reducers that push and splice the list call `invalidateTableIndexes`; any other in-place write to it must too, while a replaced list or entity map is caught by identity. An observer keeps `doc.indexIds.length` and its own indexes' fields, so with the bit set an index add or drop redraws every table and any other index change only its own (`tableKeys.test.ts` pins both).
- **Primary Key and Alt+K reach the whole column selection** once the focused column is one of two or more selected columns still in its table (`getFocusedColumnIds`, `engine/modules/editor/utils/focus.ts`), and the menu item then reads Primary Key on selected columns. A column the bare `column.remove` leaves out of its table (an undo of an add, a peer, an agent) keeps its entity and stays in `selectColumnIds`, so both the helper and `changeColumnsPrimaryKeyAction$` read only ids still in `table.columnIds`, as `pasteTableAction$` does. That generator makes every one a key while any is not and clears them all once all are, sending nothing for a column already there, since the undo of `column.changePrimaryKey` records the negation of what was sent; one column toggles as before, and `changeColumnPrimaryKeyAction$` is that rule over one column. It sends no relationship action, as the owner decided: a parent whose key columns change keeps every relationship and foreign key column its children have, still on the columns they named, while `identificationHook` still recomputes the identification of each relationship whose end columns change, as it did for one column. A right press (button 2 alone, `isSecondaryButtonPress` in `utils/domEvent.ts`; a macOS Ctrl+click is a left press) on a row keeps a selection it lands in and moves only the focus there (`$mod` on `focusColumnAction`), selects the row alone anywhere else, and focuses the row from anywhere in it outside a cell (its key, between two cells, its alternate key mark, its remove button) as from a cell (`erd/canvas/table/column/Column.tsx`), so the menu it opens never acts on a column focused before. Off the rows a right press focuses as a left one does, so one on the table outside its rows and header cells keeps the column focus it had.

**Table Properties**

- **One fixed box for all three tabs** (`erd/table-properties/TableProperties.styles.ts`): 1040 by 600 at most, hung from the palette's line 60px under the editor's top and shortened by `max-height: 100%` on a low host, so switching tabs never moves the frame. The header holds the title, a chip per table opened lately and the close button, the one control the keyboard reaches; it hands focus back with `focusEvent` on `nextTick`, or the keyboard falls to the page and the shortcuts stop. It keeps a Space keydown from the editor root, whose hand tool binding cancels it and with it the button's press. Escape and a press on the backdrop close it. The editor's `readonly` reaches it through `Erd`'s prop, as it reaches `Toolbar`: a badge after the title, no add row or remove button, names that take no typing, every box disabled and the order fixed. That is display only; the store's readonly filter stays the guard.
- **The keys and the Columns heading stick to the body's top edge** (`TablePropertiesIndexes.styles.ts`): one body scrolls the whole tab, and the left pane sticks inside `leftTrack`, which `align-self: stretch` makes as tall as the columns beside it and only as tall as its own rows once the panes stack, so the keys stay beside a long list and scroll away above one without a container query, which the browser floor lacks. A key list taller than the body shows its last rows at the end of the scroll. `columnsHead` reaches over the body padding as the order does. The browser counts a box under a sticky band as shown, so each checkbox carries a `scroll-margin` of the stuck heading and the tallest order (`INDEX_ORDER_MAX_ROWS` rows), and Tab never stops on a box the order hides; a mouse focus scrolls nothing.
- **The Index order sticks to the body's bottom edge** (`order` in `TablePropertiesIndexes.styles.ts`): it sticks 12px past the body's padding edge, pads its rows back by as much and takes that padding out of the flow with a negative margin, so a stuck order hides the rows under it and one in place adds no scroll height. Both it and `scrollbarArea` in `TableProperties.styles.ts` read that padding from `TABLE_PROPERTIES_BODY_PADDING` (`constants/layout.ts`). Its rows carry the canvas's `AK<n>.<m>` marks, numbered as `getAlternateKeyMarks` numbers them. A row's `class` stays one constant value: the drag adds `dragging` and `none-hover` by hand, and r-html strips a class it did not set whenever the bound value changes, so the read only state is the `data-readonly` attribute instead.
- **The index column checkboxes are drawn, not native** (`IndexesCheckboxColumn.styles.ts`, the editor's only `type=checkbox`): `appearance: none`, a gray-10 ring, an accent fill with a white check from a data URI, gray-9 at full opacity for a picked key's columns; forced colours hand them back to the system. A row whose box is checked carries `data-selected`, so `Column.styles`' tint and a bar tie it to the picked row on the left. happy-dom's CSSOM drops that data URI, so a unit spec reads it from the template text; Chromium keeps it.
- **Its titles are an e2e contract**: `e2e/specs/table-properties-indexes.spec.ts` finds rows by `[title="Add Index"]`, `[title="Unique"]`, `[title="Alternate Key n"]`, `[title="Remove"]` and `div:has(> [title="Read Only"])`, the lock a direct child of a key row. A new element takes none of those titles, or a strict locator matches two.

**Export image dialog**

- **Every image export goes through one dialog** (`components/export-image/ExportImage.tsx`, owner decisions of 2026-10-04): Export ▸ Image in the canvas menu and in the palette emit `openExportImage`, and `ErdEditor` mounts it beside the theme builder under `Open.exportImage`, reader-only like every `openMap` key. It holds a preview, a close button, Background, Dark mode, Scale (1x, 2x, 3x), PNG, SVG and Copy to clipboard, and nothing else. The close button (an owner decision of 2026-10-05) is the editor's 26 px `x` tool, as Table Properties and Find and Replace have it, named `common.close`, translated, with the stop key in its title, beside the title in a grid row of its own that heads the options while the preview stands beside them and the whole box once the preview stacks above them, so the button keeps the top corner the title row ends at either way, the left one under a right-to-left language, and its focus across the switch, and its `scroll-margin` brings its whole focus ring into a short box scrolled down when Tab turns round to it; it calls the handler Escape calls, and it is first in the Tab order but never the first focus. The options last for the element's life, never in storage: Background on, Dark mode seeded once from `hasDarkMode()`, 2x. A button leaves it open and answers in a toast, the toasts stacking over it: Copy to clipboard always does, PNG and SVG only while they run long or on a failure and PNG on a reduction, never for a file saved in full (an owner decision, pinned in `exportImageActions.test.ts`).
- **It is built on `primitives/dialog/Dialog.tsx`**, the shared modal: a dim over the whole editor (one z-index under the toasts) that closes on a press begun on it (a press begun in the box and let go over the dim, a text selection say, clicks the dim too and closes nothing), Escape spent inside it (prevented and stopped, so the canvas keeps its selection), Space stopped so a native button keeps its press from the hand tool's binding, the `data-autofocus` control focused on mount (here PNG), Tab turned round at either end of the box, which scrolls in a short editor, and `focusEvent` on unmount, whatever closed it. While it is open `erdShortcutPerformCheck` stops the canvas shortcuts, copy and paste, and the palette and Find and Replace close it as they close Table Properties, Find and Replace standing aside under it, still open, so its chord trades the dialog for the panel open already under it too (`isPanelShown`). It stacks the preview above the options under 640 px of editor width (`editor.viewport.width`, since the browser floor has no container query). Map Columns (below) is the dialog's second user and keeps the same contract: the canvas shortcuts, undo and redo stop while it is open, Find and Replace stands aside under it (`isPanelShown`), and Find and Replace and the palette, by their chords from inside it or any other way they open, close it and drop what was picked, as the owner decided on 2026-10-06.
- **The image is the document as the dialog opened on it**: `toJson`, the palette (scene theme, preset options, appearance) and the display language (`{ locale, messages }`, which the preview, PNG and SVG requests all carry) are fixed at opening, for the preview and for every file. Every export is drawn at 100% (an owner decision of 2026-10-06), the PNG, the SVG, Copy to clipboard and the preview alike, whatever zoom the editor shows or the document was saved with: no caller passes a zoom, `ExportScene` draws every table with `Table`, never `HighLevelTable`, and `renderDocumentScene` writes `EXPORT_ZOOM_LEVEL` (`services/export-png/exportBox.ts`) into the export's own store over the zoom the document JSON carries, so every referential action label View Option leaves shown is drawn too (`RelationshipActionLabel` hides it at a zoom of 0.7 or below). The size is the box times the scale (`pixelRatio` of `createDocumentPng`), cut by the canvas ceilings, which the dialog states before any file exists as the PNG's size (`PNG 1200 × 800 px`, since the svg ignores the scale and meets no canvas ceiling; `getExportSize` over the box the preview measured) and the reduction toast reports after in the same pixels and words, asked and written (`describeAskedSize` and `describeReduction` return the templates `localized` builds from `exportImage.reducedFrom` and `exportImage.reducedFromTo`; the toast's pixels come from the `getExportSize` the realm that drew the file runs). Dark mode matching the screen draws the scene theme, overrides included; the other appearance is the preset of the same gray and accent (`createExportTheme`). Background off makes `canvasBackground` transparent, which also clears the box under a referential action label. The preview is `createDocumentPreview`, the export's own worker path capped at `PREVIEW_MAX_SIDE` (`maxSide` in `renderDocumentScene`, which caps the scale and leaves the store at the export's 100%, so the preview draws every table and label the file does; `getStageSize` keeps the Stage a pixel thick at least, since a canvas truncates a thinner side to none and a document far longer than it is thick is cut below one), drawn again only for Background and Dark mode, 200 ms after the last toggle, cached per combination, an answer that lands after a later toggle kept but not shown (its request number), with no time limit; a drawing that fails takes the last picture away, which would contradict the switches. Copy to clipboard writes `ClipboardItem({ 'image/png': promise })` inside the click (`copyImageToClipboard`), and a refusal tells the reader to save a PNG; whether JetBrains' JCEF takes the write is unchecked.
- **SVG writes the same scene as vectors** (`services/export-png/sceneSvg.ts`, `renderSvg.ts`, owner decisions of 2026-10-04): `ExportPngService.renderSvg` draws the scene the png draws, worker first behind the same font probe, on a Stage held to 256 px since nothing rasterises it, and `toSceneSvg` walks its seven node kinds into one string, no dependency and no DOM, so the worker writes it too. The viewBox is the export box in scene units and the width and height that box too, the svg being drawn at 100% as every export is, the scale ignored and no canvas ceiling met; the shadows are left out, every font is named by its `font-family` alone (the scene face and size once on the root), a text is written in the lines konva wrapped and cut (`textArr`) on the baseline its `sceneFunc` uses, a colour with an alpha (the preset's alpha tokens are eight-digit hex, which SVG 1.1 cannot read) is written as the opaque colour plus a `fill-opacity` or `stroke-opacity` (`toPaint`), and a fill or stroke that paints nothing, the hit boxes, the hidden icons and any colour whose alpha is none, writes no element. `exportFile.ts`'s `exportSVG` hands the host an `image/svg+xml` blob named `.svg`, which every host saves whatever the extension. `sceneSvg.attributes.test.ts` reads every attribute a scene tag the export reaches can set and fails on one the writer neither writes nor names in `SVG_SKIPPED_ATTRIBUTES`, so a new attribute on a scene component is written into the svg or skipped on purpose; `renderSvg.browser.test.ts` holds the attributes konva really keeps to the same lists, and it and `sceneSvg.browser.test.ts` hold the svg's pixels, text included, to the canvas konva paints, shadows aside, since every text spec that reads attributes repeats the writer's baseline arithmetic. A memo body's line height is measured off the page, which the worker has none of (`getMemoLineHeightPx`'s fallback), so a worker export, the png's too, sets memo lines a little apart from the screen's.

**Schema SQL tab**

- **The options panel and the window's own choices** (`components/schema-sql/`, owner decisions of 2026-10-07): the tab draws the DDL of the whole document or of the tables chosen under Tables, on the left in every language (Display language, below), beside a 300 px panel (`SCHEMA_SQL_PANEL_WIDTH`) holding the Database and Bracket selects, Statements (Create, If not exists, Drop & re-create), Header (None, `USE`, `CREATE + USE`), Tables while the document lists a table group (below), the before and after script editors, and Save file and Copy at its foot; Table Properties' Schema SQL tab stays the plain code, always Create with no header or script. Statements, Header, Tables and whether the panel shows live in `schemaSQLViewOf(app)` (`schemaSQLView.ts`), a `WeakMap` over the store: never in the store, the file, a change event or storage, kept across tab switches until the element goes, one set per editor. They hold what the reader asked for, `ifNotExists` and `createAndUse` at first, which `resolveSchemaSQLOptions` turns into what each database has (a segment it lacks is `aria-disabled`, titled Not in it), so a choice outlives a change of database. The panel opens unless the editor is under 640 px wide (`SCHEMA_SQL_PANEL_COLLAPSE_BELOW`) when the tab, or its own menu, first reads a measured `editor.viewport.width` (Table Properties' Schema SQL menu leaves it unsettled), then keeps what it was set to, folded behind a Show options button over the code. Export ▸ Schema SQL, in the canvas menu and the palette, saves nothing at once: `showSchemaSQLExport` opens the tab with the panel and focuses Save file, which names the file by `exportFile.ts`'s `createName`.
- **Tables chooses what the DDL writes by table group** (`view.tables`, unsaved as an owner decision): a box for All (mixed while some are checked), one for each group in `doc.tableGroupIds` order, its name or `common.unnamed` in the placeholder's colour, a dot where `toOpaqueHex` reads its color, and No group, the tables in none. Every box checked is the tab's output with no group at all, `createSchemaSQL` given no table filter; a subset hands it the tables of the checked boxes in document order (`chosenTableIds`, a table's group read as `getTableGroupId` reads it) with `scripts: true`, so the scripts stay, a foreign key to a table left out is still written, and Drop & re-create, its warning and the Oracle long names cover those tables alone; no box checked writes `''` under a hint over the code (`schemaSql.noTablesChosen`), and Save file and Copy write what the code shows. The choice keys on group ids and is replaced whole, so a watcher hears one change: an `observer` the full tab holds while mounted matches it to the groups the document lists (`syncTableChoice`), at mount to those that changed since, a group not met starting checked only while every box was, a removed one's choice dropped, and none left checking every box again. Readonly chooses alike, since nothing of it enters the document. The text follows the choice and the group list, while a membership change alone, like any table edit, shows when the tab mounts again.
- **A script editor commits once per edit** (`schema-sql-options/ScriptEditor.tsx`): a textarea under a shiki overlay keeps a draft and dispatches `changeDDLScriptAction` on blur, or on unmount while it holds the focus (a tab switch by its shortcut), only when the draft differs both from what it held as it took the focus and from the document's, so one undo on the ERD tab takes back one edit and a field only read never writes back over a peer's or an agent's script; it takes their value while it is not focused, and on a blur with nothing typed or in a readonly editor, where it is a readonly textarea. It grows with its text, its box holding its height while the field is measured, so the scrolled panel keeps its place and the caret stays in sight. Its keydown stops at the field, without `preventDefault`, every chord of `keyBindingMap` but find, the palette, Escape and the zoom keys (`SCRIPT_PASSING_BINDINGS`), and a composition: the root's `useKeyBindingMap` cancels a chord on every tab, and no store on this tab undoes, so `$mod+Z`, a line or word delete and a macOS Option character would do nothing in it. Copy, cut and paste stop there too, away from the diagram's clipboard. The panel and the Show options button stop Space (`keepSpace`), the hand tool's key, whose binding cancels a native button's press.

**Code Generator tab**

- **The options panel** (`components/generator-code/`, owner decisions of 2026-10-09): the tab draws the whole document's code, on the left in every language, beside a 300 px panel in the Schema SQL panel's shell (`SchemaSQLOptions.styles.ts` and `SchemaSQL.styles.ts`, `keepSpace`, `resolvePanel` and `togglePanel`), which holds only settings the document already has: Language, Database, Table Name Case, Column Name Case and, for Doctrine, JPA and SeaORM alone, Bracket (`readsBracket`, `languageSettings.ts`; JPA quotes a reserved table name only under a bracket type, and joined the row by the owner's call on 2026-10-10), then Save file and Copy at its foot. No language has an option of its own there, and the panel added no document field, action or lock bit and nothing for `mcp-server`: each list dispatches the setting's own action, as the tab's context menu and the palette do. A setting the language ignores stays, dimmed, with the note Not used by the language (`code.notUsedBy`) that its list's `aria-describedby` names: the Database for DBML, AML and Mermaid, both name cases for SeaORM, DBML, AML and Mermaid (`readsDatabase`, `readsNameCases`). Each name stands over its list (`shell.column`), where the Schema SQL panel sets them side by side, since `Column Name Case` takes about 127 px in English and a row beside a 132 px list leaves a name about 116 (measured 2026-10-10). The Language list is the menu's three groups (below) with an `<hr>` between them; under a right-to-left language each name ends in U+200E (`LEFT_TO_RIGHT_MARK`), so `C#` never reads `#C`, since an isolate or a mark before the name would keep a typed letter from finding it and `dir="ltr"` on an option aligns the picked value left; the Database list writes the vendors as they are, as the Schema SQL panel does. The panel shows no Settings lock (the owner's call on 2026-10-10): no glyph, title or description marks a locked row, and its list still changes the setting on every screen while the file keeps the locked value, as the context menu and the palette do (Locks, above); the Settings tab alone shows the locks (`GeneratorCodeOptions.test.ts` holds a new document, every code setting locked, to a panel with no lock).
- **Whether the panel shows** lives in `generatorCodeViewOf(app)` (`generatorCodeView.ts`), a `WeakMap` over the store apart from the Schema SQL tab's, never in the store, the file, a change event or storage, one per editor: it opens unless the editor is under 640 px wide (`SCHEMA_SQL_PANEL_COLLAPSE_BELOW`) when the tab, or its menu, first reads a measured `editor.viewport.width`, then keeps what it was set to across tab switches, folded behind a Show options button over the code; Hide options hands the focus to Show options and Show options back to Hide options. Table Properties' Code Generator tab is one table's code with no panel, and its menu offers neither Options panel nor Save file (`full`).
- **The code is a snapshot** (`GeneratorCode.tsx`, owner decisions of 2026-10-09): written as the tab mounts and again only when a setting it is written from changes (`language`, `database`, `tableNameCase`, `columnNameCase`, `bracketType`), never on a document edit, so the text stays what the reader is looking at (`GeneratorCode.test.ts`). `database` joined those keys with the panel and the menu's Database row, which set it, as `bracketType` did for Doctrine; until then nothing on the tab set it, and the code not following it was intended.
- **Save file** (`exportCode`, `utils/file/exportFile.ts`) names the file as every export does, `<databaseName>-<time><extension>`, `unnamed-` with no database name, and hands it to the host's export path (`setExportFileCallback`). The extension is the language's (`LanguageToExtensionMap`, `languageSettings.ts`: Swift `.swift`, Zod `.ts`, JSON Schema `.json`, SQLAlchemy `.py`, GraphQL `.graphql`, Mermaid `.mmd`, …, `.txt` for a language the editor does not know), Java and JPA saving all their classes in one `.java` file as is, which works as a paste (an owner decision of 2026-10-09). The code's context menu offers Language, Database, Table Name Case, Column Name Case, Bracket (Doctrine, JPA and SeaORM), Options panel (checked while it shows) and Save file…; the palette's Database row shows on this tab too, its Bracket row only for Doctrine, JPA and SeaORM, and `Code Generator: Options panel` folds the panel or opens it. Export has no Code Generator entry (an owner decision of 2026-10-09). `e2e/specs/generator-code-options.spec.ts` drives the panel in the real element.
- **The language menu's three groups** (`menuGroups`, `generator-code-context-menu/menus/languageMenus.ts`), each ascending by name ignoring case: the languages (C#, Go, Java, Kotlin, PHP, Rust, Scala, Swift, TypeScript), the ORMs (Doctrine, Drizzle, JPA, SeaORM, Sequelize, SQLAlchemy, TypeORM) and the schemas, which hold the tables written in another schema language or library (AML, DBML, GraphQL, JSON Schema, Mermaid, Zod); the separators were the owner's call on 2026-10-09, the grouping the default put to the owner with them. A `ContextMenu.Separator` (`primitives/context-menu/context-menu-separator/`: an empty `role="separator"` div, a 1 px `border-top` in the menu's own border color, which forced colors keep, `margin: 4px 0`, as wide as a row's highlight, no text and so no message key) stands before the first row of each group past the first (`separated`), a sibling of the rows and never their wrapper, since a row closes a sibling's submenu by its parent's `data-id`, so rows inside a wrapper would leave two submenus open (`ContextMenuItem.tsx`; `ContextMenu.test.ts` pins the flat case); a press on it runs nothing and leaves the menu open. The palette and the Settings lock row read `menus`, the same rows flat, with no rule.
- **Highlighting.** `LanguageToLangMap` colours Swift with Shiki's `swift` grammar, Zod with `typescript` and JSON Schema with `json` (`shikiService.ts`). `@shikijs/langs/php` already embedded `json`, so only `swift` added bytes, about 15 KB gzip to the shiki worker (shiki 4.4.3, measured 2026-10-10). GraphQL is coloured by `services/shiki/graphqlGrammar.ts`, `@shikijs/langs/graphql`'s grammar without its `embeddedLangs` (JavaScript, TypeScript, JSX and TSX, for a `${…}` of a GraphQL string inside a script, which no generated schema holds): with them, and `swift`, the shiki worker the UMD build and `obsidian-plugin` inline grew to a 2,133,548-char data url, past Chromium's 2 MiB limit that `base64InlineWorkers` guards, and without JSX and TSX it is 1,662,396 (measured 2026-10-10). `shikiService.test.ts` fails once a shiki release changes the shipped grammar (copy it again with `embeddedLangs` dropped) and holds the colouring to the shipped grammar's.

**Relationship drawing and Map Columns**

- **The buttons beside a draw's target** (`erd/draw-target/`, owner decisions of 2026-10-06): while a relationship is drawn, the table it would end on is outlined in the preview's colour and gets two buttons on its left in every language, flipped right where the left edge has no room and moved off the minimap and the floating toolbar, the same size on screen at any zoom: Map to existing columns opens Map Columns, and + does what a press on the table does, as a press on the table still does. They are DOM over the canvas (`DrawTargetButtons.tsx`), since konva raises no hover over a table drawn by its name or under a DOM element, and a scene node would hand its press to the table under it. The target is reckoned, never hovered (`findDrawTarget.ts`): the tables read from the top of the order `CanvasScene` paints them in, so of two at one z-index the later in `doc.tableIds`; none under a memo, which takes the press; the start table only once the pointer has travelled 24 px from the press that started the draw. A clear strip from the buttons to the table (`.draw-target-gutter`) spends a main press, so the outlined table is the only one a press beside the buttons reaches. Their state is a module record keyed on `sceneKeyOf` (`drawTargetState.ts`), never an action, so nothing of it is saved, undone or sent to a peer. Touch has no hover, so the buttons answer a mouse or a pen alone and a tap on a table still ends the draw at once with new columns (an owner decision of 2026-10-06, with no real device to try a touch flow on): the overlay reads no pointer while the last pointer event it heard was a finger's (a browser sends a pointer event ahead of each mouse or pen move and never ahead of the mouse events it makes up after a tap), and a finger's `pointerdown` lets go of the pointer a mouse left, so a pan or a pinch after a tap brings no table under a stale point; it is the `pointerType` that tells a finger, never `touchstart`, which a pen on iPadOS or Android sends too, and a pen's press keeps its buttons. Readonly, the hand tool, a takeover, Table Properties, an active view and Map Columns itself show none.
- **Map Columns** (`components/map-columns/`, the pure half in `utils/map-columns/`, owner decisions of 2026-10-06) maps the parent's key onto the child's columns, a row per key column, for a new relationship from the buttons above or for one held from the relationship menu, which hides it in readonly and ends an armed draw before it opens. The key is the primary key, in table column order, or a unique column, References offering the choice only while there are two or more, a unique column over the primary key's very columns counting as it; a unique index is no key here, since the generators write a foreign key before the index it would need. A row's child is picked in a native `<select>`, prefilled only where exactly one column bears a name the parent column gives (`users_id`, the same from the table part of a schema-qualified name, and a unique column's own), never the parent column itself. Map or Save is one dispatch of `mapColumnsAction$`, so one version and one undo: the New column rows' columns as the pointer draw makes them, the data type changes the sync makes, then `relationship.add` with a new id, or `relationship.changeColumns` on the relationship edited, which keeps its id, its place, its constraint name, its type and its referential actions. A column the mapping stops using stays, NOT NULL and all, and loses its foreign key mark to the hook. The batch is judged on the state it lands on: what the document already holds writes nothing and says nothing, while a table or the relationship gone, or anything the dialog would have shown as standing in the way (rows that no longer cover the key as it stands among them), writes nothing behind a toast. Built from the document on every render, the dialog follows a peer's edit, keeping what the reader picked, says when the relationship it edits changed meanwhile, and closes with a toast once a table or the relationship it maps is gone. A stored mapping its lists cannot pair (two lengths, a repeated column) shows `(invalid)` rows and a column gone `(removed)`, either keeping Save off until a key or a pick repairs it. Its text is `i18n/messages/en/mapColumns.ts` (`mapColumns.*`, the buttons' titles and the menu row included) and the `common.*` words it shares (Cancel, Primary Key, unnamed, the notation names, which `relationshipTypeName` reads from the relationship menu's own table), read in each render, the toasts where they render (`localized`, and `TableRemovedText` for a removed table's name or its unnamed); names and data types enter as placeholders, and a right-to-left language's subtitle points its arrow left, from the parent on the right.
- **`relationship.changeColumns` holds both lists under one register**, `columns`, so a mapping never mixes two writers, and of two writes at one Lamport version the greater `JSON.stringify([start.columnIds, end.columnIds])` wins on every peer; a payload naming other tables than the relationship's is ignored before the register moves. `column.changeDataType` breaks a tie the same way, the greater type of two written at one version winning, since Map Columns writes a child's type with no one typing it and the undo of one mapping can meet another peer's at one version; a batch that writes one column's type twice so keeps the greater, not the last. An edit landing on a removed relationship changes its tombstone, so undoing the removal brings it back with that mapping, and an undo writes the replaced lists back over a later edit, as the undo of any field does. An editor built before the type drops it, keeping the old mapping until it opens the document again, and its removal of a column it still maps takes the remapped relationship from every peer; a peer that joined through the register handshake (`getLWW` / `mergeLWW`, which carries versions, not values) can skip an edit of the version it took and differ until it opens the document again. `mcp-server` emits none, its reachability list saying why. Concurrent column adds keep the order they arrive in, so peers that each added one, a Map's New column among them, list them in different orders: `engine/peer-store.converge.test.ts` asserts that order as measured, so a column order register would show there, beside every other mapping race it holds.

**Coordinates and scrolling**

- **One coordinate canon**: `getSceneOrigin`, `toScreenPoint`, `toScenePoint`, `getOriginToPlace` over `settings.originX` / `originY`. `konva/scene/coordinateAuthority.test.ts` fails on origin-with-zoom arithmetic outside its `AUTHORITY` files.
- **Pans are unclamped**: `scrollTo` / `streamScrollTo` write the origin as given and `changeViewport` moves nothing. The scroll ranges (`settings/atom.actions.ts`) serve the scrollbars and minimap alone, whose drags gate themselves via `clampScrollMovement`.
- Only a load pulls onto content: `openingOrigin` via `pullScrollIntoRange`, deferred by `editor.scrollPullPending` until a measured viewport. A store that loads before measurement first dispatches `changeViewportAction({ width: 0, height: 0 })` (`createReplicationStore`, `createPeerStore`, `ErdEditor.tsx`), or the load pulls against the default viewport for good.
- **Pinch**: `hooks/usePinchZoom.ts` is the pinch of every store scene (`Erd`, `ErdViewer`, Flow via `useViewGestures`); Graph mode drives `createPinch` (`utils/pinch.ts`) on its own view. A trackpad pinch is a ctrl wheel (`isPinchWheel`: every one on Apple, one short of a notch elsewhere, so a `$mod` notch keeps its step), a touch pinch two fingers, Safari's gesture events; each holds its centre through `pinchZoomAction$`, streamed so one pinch is one undo entry. The roots carry `touch-action: none`, or the page zooms instead. `getZoomTransform` hands back the store's own object: snapshot what a gesture holds.
- **Drags freeze their geometry**: entity drags (`canvas/entityDrag.ts`), scrollbar drags (`useVirtualScroll.ts`) and the minimap drag (`useMinimapScroll.ts`) freeze content rect and origin via `konva/scene/viewFreeze.ts`, thawing in the finalizer and on unmount; an entity drag in the document also holds each table group's box as it began (Konva scene, below). Unfrozen, a drag resizes its own thumb or map.
- **A module-level per-scene record keys on `sceneKeyOf(root, source)`** (`${editor.id}:${source}`, `konva/scene/viewFreeze.ts`), as the freeze, `entityDrag.ts`, `columnDragPointer.ts` and the draw target's `erd/draw-target/drawTargetState.ts` do; keyed on the source alone, the diff viewer's two stores or two elements on a page share a slot. Only a `WeakMap` over entities (`draw-relationship/calc.ts`, the sort channels) may key on the source alone.
- **Compatibility fields** (`erd-editor-schema` owns their rules, in its `AGENTS.md`): in this package `settings.width` / `height` are written only by the four importers and read only by `sortTableAction`, and outside it `erd-editor-schema`'s `migrateScrollToOrigin` reads them on a load that has no origin; `scrollLeft` / `scrollTop` are the frozen legacy pair it migrates into the origin once. The importer `omit` list (`IMPORT_OMIT_SETTINGS` in `editor/generator.actions.ts`) names both pairs.

**Konva scene**

- **konva only through `konva/lib/*`**: `src/konva/imports.test.ts` allowlists the shipped modules, bans the barrel, keeps `Animation` out and `Tween` in `konva/scene/konvaFlip.ts` alone. Without the barrel nothing sets `Konva.DD`, so `Reflect.set(Konva, 'DD', DD)` in `batchDraw.ts` is load-bearing: without it every stage pointer dispatch throws. `konva/scene/renderScene.browser.test.tsx` pins it; no unit spec can.
- **Scene roots export in lower case on purpose**: `renderCanvasScene` / `renderMinimapScene` render imperatively and `Canvas.tsx` / `Minimap.tsx` hand-roll `import.meta.hot.accept`. Capitalize or drop either and `vite-plugin-r-html` makes the module a component boundary; the parent's accept silently stops running.
- The export-png SharedWorker hot-swaps scene modules on the dev server only because HMR meets on `globalThis` (rule in `packages/vite-plugin-r-html`); moved to `window`, the worker draws stale modules or throws at start, and every export falls back to the main thread.
- **The PNG and SVG exports draw their own scene** from the document JSON (fresh app context, detached Stage, the editor's viewport and zoom never read), in the display language they are handed (`LocaleMessages`), English without one. No caller passes a zoom to `createDocumentPng`, `createDocumentSvg` or `createDocumentPreview`: `renderDocumentScene` dispatches `changeZoomLevelAction` with `EXPORT_ZOOM_LEVEL` once the JSON is loaded, over whatever zoom `toJson` wrote (the live one, or the one the lock holds while the viewport is locked), so every export is drawn at 100% (the Export image dialog's owner decision of 2026-10-06). `getExportScale` and `pixelRatio.ts` cut to the canvas ceilings; `export-image/exportImageActions.ts` surfaces reductions and failures.
- **`ParticleLayer.tsx`'s template stays one childless `k-layer`**: `createParticleLoop` adds particles behind the host's ledger, and a template child makes the next reconcile remove them all. It is a rAF loop on `layer.draw()` that asks no frame while nothing is lit.
- **Table groups draw first in the scene layer**, behind the connectors, by zIndex among themselves, from the document source alone (`getVisibleIds` gives a view none) and never culled (`canvas/table-group/TableGroups.tsx`, which the export scene draws first too, and the minimap's own). `TableGroups` reads the list and every group's members itself (`createTableGroupMemberLists`, which hands a group the list it had while no member came or went, and a group with none one shared list left unfrozen: r-html tracks no read of a frozen value, so a frozen one would keep a group from redrawing over its first member), so a scene render re-renders no group, and draws in two passes: every body (`TableGroupBody.tsx`, its own node `table-group-body-<id>`) before every frame (`TableGroup.tsx`: the bar, the line, the peer ring, the sashes), so no group's body stands over another's bar, as a large group drawn over a smaller one it holds would; the two share the sash draft and the double-click guard (`createTableGroupLinks`). The box is `getDrawnTableGroupRect` (`tableGroupBox.ts`), `getTableGroupRect`'s while no drag holds the scene; its top `TABLE_GROUP_TITLE_HEIGHT` is the title bar holding lucide's `group` icon at the table header icon's size and gap (`getTableGroupIconBox`, `titleLayout.ts`; a non-listening holder, so a press on it is the bar's) and the name, which the padding above a member clears, and under it the body takes the group color at `TABLE_GROUP_FILL_OPACITY`, one line framing both (`memoSelect` while selected, as a memo); a group with no color `toOpaqueHex` reads takes the theme's own group tokens, its bar `tableGroupHeaderBackground` (gray-7, gray-8 in light: two steps off a table header, so it reads as neither), its body `tableGroupBackground` (grayA-4 at full opacity, the alpha its own) and its line `tableGroupBorder` (the bar's step, as a colored group's line is its color), the icon and a name in `active`, and an unnamed one `common.unnamed`; `radix-ui-theme.test.ts` holds bar, body and canvas apart (CIE76 above 5) and the name at 7:1 in every theme. Every box asks for a redraw whenever any table's widths may have changed (`trackTableWidths`, the observable twin of the width generation `invalidateTableWidths` bumps), since a member widened by a column edit or a remeasure changes no field the size cache keys on. The title bar and the body are separate nodes, the parts a press is told apart by (next bullet). While a drag runs and leaves the group standing, the box is the one it showed as the drag began (`getHeldTableGroupBoxes`, which `beginEntityDrag` takes with the flag) united with the members the drag leaves standing, so it neither shrinks under a member lifted out nor stretches after one carried off, on the canvas and the minimap alike; a group dragged itself keeps every member. A member table's header band takes its group's color (`getTableHeaderTint`; the scene names each table's group off one read of the list, `tableGroupId`, for `getTableGroupTint`, so a group coming or going re-renders no other table), and its name, comment, placeholders, header icons and the cell editor over them black or white, whichever has the higher WCAG 2 contrast ratio (`utils/contrastText.ts`, never under 4.58, an owner decision), its own color staying on the left edge; a view, the zoomed-out table and a drawn copy (`preview`, the Alt drag's ghost, whose duplicate lands in no group) wear no tint. `Show.hideTableGroup` takes boxes, tints and their room in the content bounds away everywhere, and is masked out of the table size key and the sort hook (`isPaintOnlyToggle`), so the View Option toggle measures nothing. The minimap fills each group box in its color at the same alpha behind the marks, and each table mark in `table.ui.color` (an owner decision), never its group's.
- **A table group's pointer**: `sceneHit` answers `{ kind: 'tableGroup', part }`, the part `title`, `body` or `sash` read off the kind its node carries; the bar's and the body's fills are their hit areas, the name, line and ring listen to nothing, and a table, memo or connector over a group answers for itself. The title bar selects on a press (`selectTableGroupAction$`, the modifier adding, a press inside the selection keeping it) and carries the group past `CLICK_DRAG_MIN_MOVE` through `useMoveEntity` (`TITLE_KINDS` as its `clickKinds`), `moveAllAction$` moving it and its members once; it is never Alt duplicated, and a readonly store makes the press a selection alone (`canMove`).
