The body is canvas to the main button: `ownsPress` (`erd/hitTest.ts`), which `handleDragSelect` in `Erd.tsx` and `ErdViewer.tsx` read, gives a group its bar and sashes and its body only for another button, so a main press there pans, takes a marquee under the modifier and unselects, while a right press selects the group, kept if the selection holds it, for the group menu it raises (next bullet); a menu a main press raises, a Mac Ctrl+click's or a long touch's, selects the group as it opens (`Erd.tsx`'s `handleContextmenu`), since that press unselected. A drag of a group puts every group in the bottom layer, by zIndex as they stood, under the static scene, and its members in the drag layer (`CanvasScene.tsx`'s `dragIds` take a selected group's members); a drag of tables alone leaves the groups where they stand. `TableGroupSash.tsx` stands on a selected group's four edges and four corners, for the main button, and not under a readonly store: its drag draws a draft box in the group's body and frame and writes one `tableGroup.resize` with `dispatchSync` as it ends, the edge stopping at the members' padded box (`getTablesGroupRect`) and at `TABLE_GROUP_MIN_WIDTH` / `TABLE_GROUP_MIN_HEIGHT`, so peers hear one write and one undo puts back the rect it began from. A document drag that travelled `CLICK_DRAG_MIN_MOVE` from its press ends by dispatching `dropTablesIntoGroupsAction$` with the boxes it held (`useMoveEntity`'s finalizer; a click, or a tremor short of that, sends nothing, `markEntityDragTravelled`): `getTableGroupDrops` judges each selected table no selected group carries, putting it in the topmost box that holds its centre without them, the box a group the drag leaves standing showed as it began included, or in none, so a member grown past the stored rect stays through a nudge and leaves once its centre is out of the box the reader saw, and judges nothing while groups are hidden; the moves and the membership undo as one entry while the release comes inside the 200 ms quiet period that closes the drag's `@@move` group, and a pointer resting longer before it leaves the membership an entry of its own, as a paused drag already splits its moves. `TableGroupDropTarget.tsx` outlines each group a drop would land a table in, in the box it is judged in, in `theme.focus` in the drag layer, nothing short of the click distance and nothing under a readonly store.
- **Table group commands** (`components/erd/table-group/`, `erd-context-menu/ErdContextMenu.tsx`, `quick-search/actions.tsx`): a right press on a group's bar, body or sash raises the group menu: Select tables (`selectTableGroupTablesAction$`, its member tables alone), Rename, Color, Remove color while the selection holds a color, and Delete, which removes that group alone and keeps its tables (`removeTableGroupAction$`), or reads Delete selected while the group is one of two or more selected, as the memo's does; a readonly store gets Select tables alone. The table menu adds, above its Delete, Group selected tables (`addTableGroupFromTablesAction$`, while the selection holds a table the document lists) and Remove from group (`setTableGroupAction$(ids, '')`, while a selected table is in a group); the canvas menu adds New Table Group after New Memo, and the palette New Table Group and Group Selected Tables (with a table selected), on the ERD tab alone, each keeping its English as an alias. None shows under a readonly store, and no creating one while groups are hidden. The floating toolbar has no creation buttons, so it has none for groups.
- **The draw mode** (`editor.drawTableGroup`, written by `editor.changeDrawTableGroup`, which no action list holds, so it is never saved, sent or undone, as the hand tool's): New Table Group arms it, putting the hand down and ending a relationship draw, either of which ends it in turn, and it takes the stage off the pointer as the hand tool does (`grabMove`), so `handleDragSelect` in `Erd.tsx` hears every press under a crosshair. A main press draws (`useTableGroupDraw.ts`): the box the travel spans, never under `TABLE_GROUP_MIN_WIDTH` / `TABLE_GROUP_MIN_HEIGHT` at the zoom, shows in the erd root's pixels (`TableGroupDraft.tsx`), and the release adds `addTableGroupAction$` over it in scene units, rounded, taking in the ungrouped tables whose centre it holds, and ends the mode; a release within `CLICK_DRAG_MIN_MOVE` adds `TABLE_GROUP_DEFAULT_WIDTH` by `TABLE_GROUP_DEFAULT_HEIGHT` with its corner at the press. The middle button still pans. Any other press ends the mode, and the contextmenu it raises, or one a Mac Ctrl+click raises while armed, opens no menu. Escape ends it alone (Escape bullet below), as do the floating toolbar's Select, which marks neither tool while the mode is armed, a tab switch (`settings.changeCanvasType` off the ERD tab), groups being hidden and the element turning readonly, a drag then running adding nothing, and a readonly store never draws. Hiding groups (`settings.changeShow` setting `Show.hideTableGroup`, here, by an undo or by a peer) also lets go of every selected group and closes a name editor, and `openTableGroupNameEditor` opens none while they are hidden, since no reader could see or end either.
- **A group's name editor** (`TableGroupNameEditor` in `canvas/EditOverlay.tsx`, open while `editor.editTableGroupId` names a group the document lists and groups are shown, which `isEditingText` reads, so the canvas shortcuts stand down as for a cell): `openTableGroupNameEditor` (`table-group/tableGroupName.ts`) opens it, letting the table focus go, for a main double click on the title bar whose first press was there too (`createDoubleClickGuard`), for Rename, and on the group a draw or Group selected tables just added (`addTableGroupAndRename`, the id the batch added to `doc.tableGroupIds`), never under a readonly store. It stands over the bar's name, which hides, from `TABLE_GROUP_NAME_X` (past the icon) to `TABLE_GROUP_TITLE_PADDING` short of the bar's end, in the bar's font and weight and in the box konva centres the name in (`getTableGroupNameBox`, `canvas/table-group/titleLayout.ts`, a cell's text height on a whole pixel), so the baselines meet, `common.unnamed` its placeholder, and a colored bar's text color goes to `--active` and `--placeholder` on its cell, as a tinted header's does. It writes once: Enter or a blur commits a changed name as one `tableGroup.changeName`, one undo entry, and a name left as it was writes nothing; Escape raises a flag before the stop branch's focus change blurs the input, so that press closes it writing nothing. The name goes in as it opens rather than as a binding, so a peer's rename leaves what is being typed.
- **Closed shadow root** (r-html's `queryShadowSelector` / `closestElement` cross it) over one `<canvas>`: route presses through `components/erd/hitTest.ts`, which records what konva resolved at dispatch, since the press re-renders the scene before `Erd.tsx` asks. Hit boxes come from hit functions (`canvas/sceneHit.ts`), not invisible shapes.
- An overlay over the canvas joins the `canDrag` `closest()` list in `Erd.tsx` (`.content-compass`, `.floating-toolbar`, `.draw-target`, …; `ErdViewer.tsx` has its own) or a press on it starts a pan; one a takeover lays over its scene joins `TAKEOVER_CHROME` there too, or a middle press on it is prevented.
- A header press lifts the table only past `CLICK_DRAG_MIN_MOVE` (`clickKinds`, `canvas/useMoveEntity.ts`); the drag layer is off the hit canvas, so lifting at once keeps a double click from opening the cell editor.
- **The middle button pans and only the main button carries a table, memo or Graph mode dot** (an owner decision of 2026-10-04, the dot of 2026-10-05). The middle button pans from anywhere: `listenMiddleButtonPan` (`canvas/middleButtonPan.ts`), which `Canvas.tsx` binds on the stage container in the capture phase, prevents and stops the press before konva or the scene root hears it, prevents its lift wherever it lands (`preventMiddleLift`, `utils/domEvent.ts`, since a lift left unprevented is where Chromium on Linux pastes the selection, into a cell editor the prevented press left focused) and stops that lift over the scene, so no table, memo, sash, cell, icon or connector selects, drags or clicks for it (konva makes a click of any button's press and lift, and opens its double click window on any lift) and the selection stays; it hands the press to `drag$` with `forwardMoveStartEvent`, since the window never hears it and the first step would jump, ends the pan itself on the lift it stops, pans through `sceneStreamScrollToAction`, holds the grabbing cursor (`holdStageCursor`, `utils/stageCursor.ts`, the hold `holdSceneCursor` and Graph mode share) and closes the context menu of the root it stands in and, in the document scene, the colour picker; the theme builder and the locale picker (`handlePress` in `ErdEditor.tsx`) and the Flow's Row display menu (`VisualizationToolbar.tsx`), which close on a press that bubbles up to them, hear it as the forward (`middlePanPress$`, in `utils/globalEventObservable.ts`, since `canvas/sceneOwnership.test.ts` lists every reference into the canvas root from outside it), as they hear a main press there. The edit overlay, the scrollbars, the minimap and the toolbars sit beside that container and keep their own presses. Under the hand tool the controller takes no pointer and the root pans for any button, a middle press by the same rule (`handleDragSelect` in `Erd.tsx`): it keeps the selection, draws no marquee whatever modifier is held, and is prevented with its lift (`preventMiddleLift`), so the middle button reads alike in every mode (an owner decision of 2026-10-04). Under a takeover (the diff viewer, time travel, the automatic placement preview: `isTakenOver`, `find-replace/panelLayout.ts`) a middle press over its scene reads the same way (an owner decision of 2026-10-04): it leaves the document's selection under it and is prevented with its lift (`handleDragSelect` in `Erd.tsx`, which every takeover stands in). Time travel and the preview pan for no button; a diff pane, whose pan the press still drives, keeps its own selection for it too (`ErdViewer.tsx`). The chrome over a takeover's scene (the diff tree, `.diff-viewer-tree`; the time travel slider, `.time-travel-slider`; a pane's compass, minimap and scrollbars) keeps its own presses as this canvas's does (`TAKEOVER_CHROME` in `Erd.tsx`): a middle press there goes unprevented and takes the selection off as a main press does, so the diff tree, which scrolls, keeps the browser's middle autoscroll. A main press anywhere over a takeover takes the document's selection off. Graph mode reads the button in `GraphNode` and `VisualizationScene` through `captureGraphPan`, which prevents a middle press and its lift the same way; a dot is pinned and moved for the main button alone (`isMainButtonPress` in `GraphNode`), so a right press and its drag on one do nothing, while the background still pans for any button. Each of these prevents the default that focuses the editor root on any other press, so the root takes the keyboard on a middle press itself (`handlePress` in `ErdEditor.tsx`, which hears the canvas's as the forward, through `checkAndFocus`, which leaves a caret already inside the element where it is). Only the main button carries a table or memo (`isMainButtonPress` in `useMoveEntity`): a right press keeps a selection it lands in and otherwise selects what it lands on alone, whatever modifier is held, so the context menu acts on what it shows, and moves nothing, nor does a memo's edge resize for it (`MemoSash.tsx`). Only the main button clicks a scene button either (the header +/x, a remove icon, a colour edge, the Flow's Related and Go to ERD; an owner decision of 2026-10-04) or opens a memo's body editor, where a right click opens the context menu alone (an owner decision of 2026-10-05): konva makes a click of any button's press and lift, so `sceneIcon`, every colour edge (`Table`, `HighLevelTable`, `Memo`) and the memo body (`Memo`) wrap their click in `mainButtonClick` (`canvas/sceneTokens.ts`). A Mac Ctrl+click is button 0 with control held, a main press to both helpers (an owner decision of 2026-10-04): each asks for button 0 alone, so every other button, the right one included, is no main press, and on a Mac a Ctrl+click on a memo's body opens its editor beside the context menu Chromium raises for that click.
- `YIELDS_TO_A_CARET` (`hooks/useKeyBindingMap.ts`) stands `selectAllTable`, `handTool` and `removeSelection` down in a text field, per binding, because `Enter` / `Escape` must still reach `handleShortcut` from the cell editor. `findReplace` stays out of it: in a field `$mod+F` means only the page's find, which the editor's replaces. `YIELDS_TO_A_TAKEOVER` beside it leaves `findReplace` unprevented under a takeover (`isTakenOver`) alone, on whichever tab.
- **Escape is two presses**, decided in the `stop` branch of `erd/useErdShortcut.ts` from the state it arrives in. An open cell, memo or group name editor, a relationship being drawn or an armed table group draw takes the press alone (`editTableEndAction` / `editMemoEndAction` / `editTableGroupEndAction` / `drawEndRelationshipAction` / `changeDrawTableGroupAction`, the relationship draw taking the buttons beside its target with it; focus, selection and typed text stay) and the branch calls `preventDefault`, so a host that skips a prevented Escape (Obsidian's workspace, a page's own dialog) leaves it be; any other Escape unselects and goes on unprevented. The memo textarea has no Escape handler of its own: a trusted press drains microtasks between listeners, so its blur would end the edit before the branch looked, which only a real key shows (`erd/useErdShortcut.browser.test.tsx`). A composing Escape never reaches `shortcut$` (`useKeyBindingMap`).
- **Delete and Backspace remove the selection** (`KeyBindingName.removeSelection`, in the `erd/useErdShortcut.ts` branch beside `removeTable`): the focused table's selected columns while that table is the whole selection and the zoom draws its rows (`getRemovableColumns`, `engine/modules/editor/utils/focus.ts`), else every selected table, memo and table group, in one dispatch and so one undo, with no confirmation, and never a relationship as its target (owner decisions); the relationships of a removed table or column go with it, as under `$mod+Delete` and `Alt+Delete`, which keep their own targets. A table, memo, table group or column that an undo, a peer or an agent removed stays selected and counts for nothing: `getRemovableColumns` reads only the columns still in the table and the tables, memos and table groups still in the document, and `removeTableAction$`, `removeMemoAction$` and `removeTableGroupAction$` leave such ids out of the selection they remove, since a removal recorded for one makes an undo that brings it back. A held key removes once (`event.repeat` is dropped): a column removal hands the focus to the column above, or to the table name once the first column goes, so a repeat would run on up the columns and then take the table. The table menu's last row follows the press that raised it (an owner decision): Delete column, or Delete columns for two or more, the key's removal, only when the press landed on the row of one of those columns (`SceneHit.columnId`, `erd/hitTest.ts`), since a right press off the rows keeps the column focus; Delete selected while the table is one of two or more selected; else Delete, the table alone. The memo menu, Color, Remove color while it shows, then Delete, reads Delete selected and removes every selected table, memo and table group while the memo is one of two or more selected, else deletes that memo alone (owner decisions). A Delete row has no icon and shows the key's first chord, and the key closes an open menu, whose entity it may just have taken. A right click on a memo's body, colour bar or remove button acts only through the memo menu, since only the main button clicks there (`mainButtonClick`, in the middle button bullet above).
- **A color comes off the way the picker puts it on**: the picker's No color row (`primitives/color-picker/ColorPicker.tsx`, the last row of its panel, whichever bar or menu opened it) and Remove color in the table, memo and group menus both dispatch `removeColorAllAction$`, which clears every selected table, memo and table group that has a color in one stream group, so one undo, and sends nothing for one without; Remove color shows under Color only while the selection holds a color (`hasColoredSelection`, `engine/modules/editor/utils/color.ts`), as the owner decided. It has no icon. No color also closes the picker, which would otherwise show the color just cleared. `changeColorAllAction$` likewise sends nothing for a table, memo or table group whose color is already that string, so a color pressed again adds no undo entry while one an undo took back is painted again; it compares them ignoring letter case, so a `#3B82F6` the old picker wrote counts as the picker's `#3b82f6`, while a `#3b82f680` or an `rgb()` is still painted.
- **The color picker is the editor's own** (`primitives/color-picker/`, no color library; its layout after uiwjs/react-color's Sketch, no alpha, Radix step 9 presets, a row of the document's colors and a pipette only where the browser has one are owner decisions of 2026-10-05): a saturation and brightness area; a row of the pipette, the hue slider and a preview; Hex, R, G and B fields; sixteen presets in two rows of eight (`presetColors.ts`, the light step 9 of `Palette` in `themes/radix-ui-theme.ts`, since a document's color is the same under either theme); up to eight colors the document's tables, memos and table groups carry, under a Document colors caption shown only when there is one (`getDocumentColors`, `engine/modules/editor/utils/color.ts`: the most used first, a tie in the order they first appear, tables, then memos, then groups, read once as it opens); and No color. It reads the color it opens on, and text committed in its Hex field, through `resolveColor` (`resolveColor.ts`): `toOpaqueHex` first (`utils/tableColor.ts`: a hex of 3, 4, 6 or 8 digits, `rgb()`, `rgba()`, `hsl()` or `hsla()`, any alpha dropped), then any other color the browser reads (an owner decision of 2026-10-05), through a canvas 2d `fillStyle` set over two grounds, a value it cannot read leaving each ground as it was: a name such as `red`, an `hwb()` or a system color by the hex or `rgba()` it writes back, and one it writes back in its own syntax (`oklch()`, `lab()`, `lch()`, `oklab()` and `color()` as they are, `color-mix()` and a relative color as a `color()`) by the sRGB pixel it paints on a blank canvas, a translucent one drifting by up to one in a channel, since that pixel is stored premultiplied. An empty color, one neither reads, and one the canvas writes back in its own syntax that paints fully transparent show no value and check no swatch; a hex, `rgb()` or `hsl()` with zero alpha, and `transparent`, show their opaque channels. Opening writes nothing: a name stays in the document until a change in the picker writes its hex. The document colors row and the DBML and AML exports read `toOpaqueHex`'s forms alone, so a name is left out of both. It hands `onChange` an opaque lower-case `#rrggbb` and keeps hue and saturation unrounded in its own state (`colorModel.ts`), since a gray's channels hold no hue and black's no saturation either. A drag, a slider's key step or a hex typed whole (six or eight digits, applied as it is typed) hands a color on only when the hex changes, and a press, a swatch, a committed field or the pipette always does, which `changeColorAllAction$` (above) turns into nothing for a color already there. A field shows what is typed until Enter, its change or its blur, and Enter or its change commits the text only when it differs from what the field showed, so a field left alone sends nothing, and text that reads as no color gives way to the color again. It reads `color`, `x` and `y` once, as it mounts: `openColorPicker` on a picker already up closes it and mounts it again on the next tick (`Erd.tsx`), and nothing the document does meanwhile reaches it (an undo from the toolbar or from the key it passes on, a peer's edit, an agent's), so it can show a color the selection no longer has, which a press paints again. The pipette shows where `EyeDropper` exists and goes for the rest of that opening, handing its focus, if it held it, to the hue, once it fails with an `OperationError`, or with an `AbortError` it did not ask for within `EYEDROPPER_DEAD_MS` (150 ms), as an embedder with no chooser behind the API answers; closing the picker aborts it. It takes the keyboard as it opens and hands it back with `focusEvent` on the tick after it unmounts, however it closes. `stop` (Escape) pressed inside it closes it, prevented and stopped, so the selection stays; an Escape the editor root hears (`shortcut$`) closes it unless the palette, a cell, memo or group name editor or a relationship or table group draw takes that press, as Find and Replace reads it, while one a panel outside `Erd` spends first, Find and Replace's own Escape that closes its panel among them, leaves it open, and `removeSelection` or `removeTable` closes it, since either may just have taken what the picker paints, each press still doing what it does (`handleColorPickerShortcut`, `Erd.tsx`); a press elsewhere in `Erd` closes it too, `Erd.tsx` and `ErdViewer.tsx` telling a press inside it by its root class `color-picker`. A keydown inside stops at the panel when it is a `keyBindingMap` chord outside `PANEL_PASSING_BINDINGS` or an arrow or Tab, as in Find and Replace, so Space and Enter press a button rather than reach the hand tool and `edit` bindings; such a chord is also cancelled, as the editor root cancels it, except in a field or as a button's Space or Enter, so a host page neither scrolls nor selects all, while undo, redo, search, Find and Replace and the zoom still reach the editor, none of them closing the picker. Tab goes round inside it, each swatch group one stop whose arrows move and select as radio buttons do. A color change is a stream action (`StreamRegroupColorActionTypes`), so a drag, a held key or a run of swatch steps undoes as one entry until 200 ms pass without a change. A wheel is spent on it, and a contextmenu (a field keeps the browser's), touchstart, copy or paste stops there. Every control shows its focus in `--input-active`, a `:focus-visible` ring or a field's `:focus` border, since `reset.styles.ts` takes the outline off buttons and fields; its styles are scoped `css` alone (`ColorPicker.styles.ts`). Its root is `dir="ltr"`, so the area, the hue and the fields keep their places under a right-to-left language, while its screen reader text and preset names are translated (`colorPicker.*`). `ColorPicker.test.ts` mounts the real picker, and `Erd.test.ts`'s `Erd - color picker` presses its keys through `useKeyBindingMap` mounted around `Erd`, the path a real key takes to `shortcut$`.
- **Find and Replace** (`components/find-replace/`, `KeyBindingName.findReplace`, `$mod+KeyF`) stands over the ERD tab alone, stands aside, still open, while Table Properties, the theme builder, the locale picker, the export image dialog or Map Columns is up, yet opening it closes each, as `$mod+K` does for the palette, so the chord pressed while Table Properties is up trades that dialog for the panel (an owner decision, pinned in `FindReplace.test.ts` and, as a press the binding takes, in `hooks/useKeyBindingMap.test.ts`), and takes that tab when opened elsewhere, like every jump from outside the canvas (`erd/goToErdTarget.ts`: the tab in a dispatch of its own, then the scroll, selection and focus ring; the selection, `selectTableAloneAction$`, leaves out `selectTableAction$`'s relationship-draw branch, so a jump with a draw armed adds nothing, and Go to ERD and the palette's `#` rows select through it too; the jump lands clear of the panel by `coveredWidth` (`find-replace/panelLayout.ts`), read from the state once the ERD tab is up rather than from the panel's box, so a jump from the palette, or one that brings the panel back with the ERD tab, keeps clear of it as its own Next does; on a narrow canvas it lands in the strip beside the panel whenever that strip is at least `MIN_CLEAR_WIDTH`, 160 px, a jump's 40 px `EDGE_MARGIN` on each side and the start of a name between them (an owner decision, pinned in `panelLayout.test.ts`), and on a narrower strip it lands as if there were no panel). Its first opening in an element searches table and column names alone, Table comments, Column comments and Memos off (`DEFAULT_FIND_FIELDS`, `utils/find-replace/findMatches.ts`; an owner decision, pinned in `FindReplace.test.ts`), since a comment or memo is prose a rename should not reach unasked; from then on the panel keeps the scopes and options last set for the element's life, an opening with no query (the chord, the toolbar button, the palette row, the context menu) included. A query handed over sets the default options and the scopes it names (Palette prefixes below), so the `:` hand-off turns both comments and Memos on, and `findMatches` given no fields still reads every kind. A rect too big for the screen is scrolled to by its focus: `scrollTableIntoView`, which Go to ERD shares too, keeps a tall table where it is while its name shows and otherwise brings its header a margin below the top, or at a zoom that draws the name alone, its middle. Across, a row or a table wider than the canvas left clear goes by the cell the jump rings, as the cell editor lays it out (`getColumnFocusRect`, and the comment cell for a table comment): a comment found at the right end of a wide row lands a margin inside, where the row's left edge would leave it past the canvas or under the panel. A ringed cell itself too wide to show whole a margin inside that canvas, a long comment (`maxWidthComment` is unlimited by default) or name, goes by the match in it instead (`ErdTarget.range`, which `toErdTarget` fills from the match: measured with the store's `toWidth` from the cell's left edge, where the scene starts its text, less the padding that measure adds, and cut at the cell's right edge, where the text is cut off), since by the cell's start a match at the end of a comment wider than the canvas stays past its right edge, its cell ringed and the text found unseen; a range found in a hidden comment is dropped, the name being ringed in its place. Its height ends 16 px above the floating toolbar, which is drawn whenever the panel is, zen mode included, so on a short canvas the list gives up its height rather than cover the toolbar's tools, but never below what its controls need (`layout.floor`, the controls above the list measured by a `ResizeObserver`, plus `PANEL_CHROME_HEIGHT`): on a canvas too short for both, the count and Previous and Next stay whole down to the 16 px margin, over the toolbar where the two meet, since CSS alone cannot floor the box at its controls (Chrome's block-axis min-content of the panel is its whole list). Its keydown stops at the panel only what the canvas would act on, a `keyBindingMap` chord outside `PANEL_PASSING_BINDINGS` (`utils/keyboard-shortcut/index.ts`, shared with the color picker: `findReplace`, `search`, `undo`, `redo`, the zoom) or an arrow or Tab: with the matched cell ringed, Enter would open the cell editor and `Alt+Backspace` remove the column. Every other press goes on, since a VS Code webview hears save or the command palette only through a keydown bubbling to its window. Escape closes the panel from inside it, and from the canvas through `stop` unless a cell editor, a draw or the palette takes that press. The default chord is every host's find (an owner decision), taken on every tab from the canvas and from any field in the element alike: prevented, so a browser opens no find bar over the editor, and stopped, so a VS Code webview, which hears a key only through a keydown bubbling to its window, never hands it to the workbench; the extension leaves VS Code's own find widget off (no `enableFindWidget`), and Obsidian's view scope lets it past Search current file, which the smoke presses it through. Pressed while the panel is shown it brings the caret back to the find field with its query selected, as every host's find does, and never closes it; with the palette open, the palette gives way (`toggleSearchAction`), as its own row does; a cell or memo editor keeps it, as it keeps `$mod+K`, the press still prevented. Under a takeover (the diff viewer, time travel, the automatic placement preview) it yields to the host's find, neither prevented nor stopped (an owner decision): `yields` in `useKeyBindingMap` reads `isTakenOver` (`find-replace/panelLayout.ts`) for every `YIELDS_TO_A_TAKEOVER` binding, since the panel would open on nothing there, while the dialogs that only stand it aside (Table Properties, the theme builder, the locale picker, the export image dialog, Map Columns) still take the chord. On Visualization, Schema SQL, Code Generator and Settings it opens the panel and brings the ERD tab up, as the toolbar button and the palette row do, which name the chord on every tab; a browser's find does not search the SQL, code and settings those tabs show as text (an owner decision of 2026-10-03). Whether a JetBrains keymap takes it before JCEF is unchecked; the toolbar button, the palette row and the context menu open the panel either way. The toolbar button (`toolbar/Toolbar.tsx`, right after Search, named Find in a read-only editor) shows on every tab, since opening takes the ERD tab, so the bar never shifts as the tab changes; under a takeover it keeps its place dimmed (`disabled`, at the 0.4 opacity of the panel's own disabled buttons) and opens nothing, as `open` returns there (an owner decision). A click opens the panel or, shown already, brings the caret back to its find field, as the chord does; it never closes it. Every opening with no query does that while the panel is shown (the `openFindReplace` handler), the toolbar's, the palette row's and the context menu's, keeping the current match and the Replace run, and the button stops its press before the bar's unselect, which would take the ring off that match. Replace all is one `dispatchSync` of the atom actions `utils/find-replace/replaceActions.ts` builds (one history entry, one batch to every peer), read from a search run again at the press, never from the list on screen; it reports the matches whose replacement changed their text (`toReplaceActions`' `replaced`), so one written back as it was counts for none and a press that changes nothing says `No changes`. Replace carries the jump to the next match in its own dispatch, found in the text the replacement leaves (`rematchField`), because a scroll is an undo entry of its own: dispatched apart, the first undo after a Replace whose next match is off screen would take back only the scroll. r-html flattens every generator of a batch against the state before it, so that jump (`showNextMatchAction$`, `find-replace/matchTarget.ts`) measures a table the replacement writes in from `withReplacement`: the field's new text at the width its reducer gives it, and a fresh table object, since the table size cache is keyed on none of a table's column widths; measured on the state before, a replacement that widened the table left the next match off screen, and unscrolled. A run of Replace presses keeps where it began (`nextReplace`, `ReplaceRun` in `findMatches.ts`): once it has wrapped past the last match and comes back there it stops with `No more matches`, where it would replace the text it wrote (`Customer` to `Big Customer` grew on every press), and that place moves with a replacement written before it in its field; a jump, a change of query, options or scopes, an opening (one with no query while the panel is shown only brings the caret back) and a Replace All start a new run. A Replace pressed past the stop holds it, saying `No more matches` again and going nowhere, rather than round to the text the run wrote, until one of those starts a new run (Next, Previous and a row are jumps); an edit of the Replace field starts none, since which matches a run went over does not depend on what it writes. The run knows its field by entity and by the text it left there, its slot found again at each press (`resumeRun`): a table, column or memo a peer adds or removes before it moves that slot, and a run held by its slot never came back and grew `Customer` again. A field gone, or holding a text the run did not leave, as after an undo or a peer's edit, starts a new run at the press, so a match an undo gives back is replaced on the way round, not left behind a `No more matches`, while one given back in another field the run went over stays as it is, since only the run's own field is checked. Nothing new enters the action lists, so `mcp-server` has nothing to declare; its `erd_list` search is its own, a word ranking for paging, not this substring match.
- **When the panel searches** (`FindReplace.tsx`): its store subscription searches again, 100 ms after the last batch, only on a batch holding one of `FindTextActionTypes` (`utils/find-replace/findMatches.ts`: the adds and removes of tables, columns and memos, the name and comment changes of tables and columns, a memo's value, `table.sort`, `column.move` and the document loads), never on `ChangeActionTypes`, which holds the scrolls, zooms and moves, nor on a hover, a selection or a jump, and never on the batch of its own Replace or Replace All (`dispatchOwn`). An edit made while the panel stands aside, still open (another tab, Table Properties, the theme builder, the locale picker, the export image dialog, Map Columns, a takeover), leaves it `stale`, and the batch that shows it again searches at once, so it never shows a list the document has left behind; an opening searches once, whatever was edited while it was away. A Replace searches the document once, at the press, and lists what the text it left holds from `rematchField`, which equals a second search. What a press said (`Replaced 5 matches`, `No changes`, `No more matches`) gives way to the count once an edit's search runs (`searchEdited`), so an undo of a Replace All shows the matches it gave back counted, not the press's line over them. Typing in regex mode searches once the input pauses for `REGEX_INPUT_DELAY` (150 ms); an Enter, Next, Previous, Replace or Replace All pressed sooner searches what is typed first (`searchPending`), as a change of search, so a Replace pressed then shows what it would replace, as in plain text, rather than replace a match the last query kept current; plain text searches on each keystroke, as it is linear. All of these are owner decisions. A result row clicked before the pause searches what is typed too (`goToRow`) and goes to the match the row named if what is typed still finds it, to none otherwise, rather than to a match of the last query that the pause would then let go; a pause reached while the panel is closed or stands aside searches nothing (`searchTyped`), leaving what is typed to the opening or the batch that shows the panel again, as a change of search. A regular expression that backtracks catastrophically holds the main thread until its search finishes; the panel has no time budget (an owner decision of 2026-10-02), and a search in a worker is the separate fix left open.
- **Find and Replace's matcher** (`utils/find-replace/matcher.ts`) compiles every pattern in unicode mode alone: `toUnicodeSource` keeps an escaped letter, digit or syntax character, writes `\-` as `\x2d` and any other escaped character as its `\u{…}` code point, so the `\_` and `\-` people type in names parse, and takes a stray `]` and a brace no quantifier owns as themselves (an owner decision); every other form unicode mode refuses (`[\w-.]`, `\z`) reads as an invalid pattern. A regular expression also takes `m`, never `s`: `^` and `$` read at each line of a memo, a CRLF one too, a dot stays on its line and `[\s\S]` reaches across. Never fall back to compiling without `u`: legacy mode reads `\p{L}` as text and matches half an emoji, which a replacement then writes into the document as an unpaired surrogate. Whole Word reads as VS Code and JetBrains read it (an owner decision): an edge of a match is a boundary where a non-word character stands beside it or at that edge of the match itself, so `user ` finds `user name`, `.id` finds `user.id` and `-` finds `a-b`, while `user` still never matches inside `username` or `superuser`. It wraps the checked source as `(?:(?<!W)|(?!W))(?:…)(?:(?!W)|(?<!W))`, every group non-capturing so `$1` and `$<name>` keep their numbers, so the engine weighs the boundary while it matches (a filter after it lost `users` to `user|users`), the source compiled alone first so the wrapper never balances `user)|(id`. A word character `W` (an owner decision) is a letter, digit or mark of any script, a connector punctuation (the underscore among them) or a joiner (ZWNJ, ZWJ), after UTS 18's `\w`: a virama, a Thai vowel sign or a decomposed accent stays inside its word, and a Persian ZWNJ splits none.
- **With no prefix the palette lists commands alone** (an owner decision): the empty input and every keyword list only what the tab offers (`scopeBase`), never a table, column, comment or memo row and never a hand-off to Find and Replace; the document is searched only under a prefix. `createScopeActions` still ends with a jump per table, which only `#` hands to Fuse, a table row's `Table` keyword kept out of Fuse's keys (`tableId`), since it fuzzes to most words. Every keystroke runs Fuse over the level's whole list (`scopeBase`), as VS Code's palette does, never inside its last hits (an owner decision, pinned in `QuickSearch.test.ts`), with no prefix, under one and in a submenu alike: what a word lists owes nothing to what was typed before it, so `auto` typed over `qqqq` finds Auto Layout and a Backspace from `users` to `u` lists what `u` pasted lists. When no command listed holds the keyword, as typed or by its Hangul letters (`keywordHolder`), the list ends with `createPrefixActions`: one row per scope that searches the document (`#`, `@`, `:`), whose `insert` types its prefix before the keyword (`users` becomes `#users`), so the arrows and Enter reach it like any row. Fuse runs at `SEARCH_THRESHOLD` 0.4 (`quick-search/actions.tsx`, an owner decision), where fuse.js's default 0.6 fuzzed most words to some command (`memo` to Import and the relationships, `#us` to orders, `users` to Zero One and Zero N); 0.4 still takes a near typo (`tabel` to New Table, `imprt` to Import) and lets go of one no closer than an unrelated command (`talbe`, `mmeo`), as `actions.test.ts` pins. Fuse also scores where a hit starts (location 0, distance 100, fuse.js's defaults), so at 0.4 a word starting past index 40 of a long table name fell out of `#`, and with it the hand-off past the cap; `searchActions` therefore adds after Fuse's hits every row holding a keyword without Hangul as typed that Fuse let go (`heldPastFuse`), as the Hangul ranking adds what its letters spell. `useColumnCell.ts`'s data type hints keep their own Fuse at the default. A Fuse hit alone still does not count, since Fuse fuzzes some words to commands that do not hold them (`tables` to New Table, Database and New Table Group). The `No commands match` line (`.quick-search-empty`) shows above those rows when Fuse finds no command at all, so the list holds the prefixes alone (`orders`, `users`, typed or pasted); a word Fuse fuzzes only to commands that do not hold it lists them above the prefixes with no line (`tables`). The empty input keeps its hint line. The field rows of `@` and `:` (`createFieldActions`) are a substring search over the column names, or the comments and memos, rebuilt from the document on each keystroke. A keyword holding Hangul also matches by its letters (`quick-search/hangul.ts`), in the commands and in every scope: a text holds it when its jamo hold the keyword's, so each step a Korean IME hands over (`ㅅ` `사` `상` `사요` `사용`) still finds 사용자, and a keyword of initial consonants alone (`ㅅㅇㅈ`, spaces ignored) when the initials of its syllables do. A cluster reads as the two consonants it joins on both sides (`ㅄ` as `ㅂㅅ`, `ㄲ` stays whole), since a Windows IME composes `ㅂ` then `ㅅ` into `ㅄ`, which no syllable has as its initial, so the literal `getChoseong(text).includes(query)` of the owner's rule would drop 부서 at that step; a text stored decomposed (NFD, as macOS names files) is composed first, its highlight covering each syllable's every jamo. Fuse's rows rank whole, then from the start, then inside, then Fuse's own looser hits; the field rows list those holding the keyword as typed first, in document order as a keyword without Hangul lists them, then those only its letters spell by the same three tiers, so the cap cuts only those. A highlight widens to the whole syllables a jamo reaches. A keyword without Hangul takes none of this letter matching or ranking. The forms are spelled once per text and kept for one open of the palette, since it reads every field again on each keystroke: `clearHangulForms` drops them as it opens, closes and unmounts, so however large the document no text's forms are spelled twice in one open, and none is kept once the palette is gone. Where a field row's Hangul hit lies (`hitRange`), which centres the row's snippet and the jump's range, spells its text again, so `createFieldActions` reads it only for the rows it shows, at most `SCOPED_ACTION_LIMIT` a keystroke; `scopedActions.test.ts` bounds every spelling of the document's texts over 200 Korean tables by that count rather than timing the search, which a loaded machine stretches. A key the IME still holds (`isComposing`, read as Find and Replace and the canvas read it) moves and picks nothing: an arrow or Enter pressed mid-syllable is the IME's, and Chrome on a Mac sends that Enter again once the syllable is done. `quick-search/QuickSearch.browser.test.ts` and `e2e/specs/find-replace.spec.ts` type through Chromium's own IME over CDP (`Input.imeSetComposition`, `Input.insertText`), the one way a spec sees the steps a real composition hands over; a key pressed during such a composition reaches the input with `isComposing` set, as a real one does. Table and field rows show on every tab and take the ERD tab when picked; a table row parks its table `START_X`, `START_Y` in from the corner, scaled by the zoom (`quick-search/actions.tsx`), or just past an open Find and Replace panel, at `x = coveredWidth`, in the strip a narrow canvas leaves beside it too; no Focus row exists (AC-52). The Find and Replace row and the hand-off row past the cap leave the list under a takeover (`isTakenOver`), where the panel opens on nothing.
- **Palette prefixes** (`quick-search/paletteQuery.ts`, `scopedActions.tsx`), VS Code's Cmd+P grammar, read at the top level only (a submenu filters its own rows) and only as the first character past any space typed before it (read as a keyword, ` #users` would reach the prefix rows, which type a second prefix before it), an optional space after it: `#` tables, `@` column names, `:` table comments, column comments and memos (an owner decision of 2026-10-03; a double quote reads as plain text), `?` the prefixes as rows whose `insert` types one in and keeps the palette open. `@users.em` splits on the first dot, the table part a substring of the table name; `@users.` lists every column of those tables. With no keyword a scope lists all its rows, `:` only the non-empty texts; a quote is searched as typed. The full-width forms a Japanese IME or a Chinese one in its punctuation mode types count as what they stand for (`WIDE_PREFIXES`: `＃` `＠` `：` `？`, `．` and `。` as the dot), as the first character only, like the ASCII ones. A scoped list caps at `SCOPED_ACTION_LIMIT`, and past it a row hands Find and Replace the query with that scope's toggles on (`openFindReplace`'s `fields`), the table part of `@table.col` left behind since the panel has none, named with the count the panel opens on (every occurrence in those kinds, default options); a query handed over resets the panel's options and scopes to match. Find and Replace stays literal, so that row shows only when the cap leaves out a match holding the keyword as typed, read for `#` in the table's name rather than its row, which says `unnamed` for a table without one, and counts only those: a list held by Hangul letters alone (`ㅅㅇㅈ`, `사요`) offers none, and what those letters alone find past the cap is left out with no row saying so. The empty input shows the prefixes as a clickable hint line, and a scope shows as a label after the input, on its right or, under a right-to-left language, its left, which leaves the caret where it was.

**Display language**

- **The option lives on the element, never in the document** (owner decisions of 2026-10-06). `useErdEditorAttachElement.ts` holds it (`localeState.option`: a code, `system`, or null until `setLocale` or a pick sets one) and `resolveLocaleOption()` gives the one in force: that option, else `system` while `enableLocalePicker` is on and `en` while it is off, so an element no host configures keeps the English it always showed whatever the browser's language. `system` is `resolveLocale` over the tag `setSystemLocale` named, else over `navigator.languages`, which `hooks/useNavigatorLanguages.ts` copies on mount and on `languagechange`, since Chromium's array is frozen and r-html's `observable` never tracks it. `setLocale` and `setSystemLocale` apply before they return and `onBeforeMount` resolves again, so a language set, or a picker turned on, before `appendChild` is in the first frame; toggling `enableLocalePicker` re-applies. A `setLocale` value that is no option is ignored. Read the option in force through `resolveLocaleOption()`, never `localeState.option`, which is null before a call; the picker's check and the palette's read it so. `changeLocale` (`{ locale }`, the option picked) fires for a pick from the picker or the palette alone, the option already in force included, and never for a setter. The language enters no store, history, peer or file; each host keeps the option where it keeps the theme.
- **One `I18n` object, changed in place.** `ErdEditor.tsx` provides `localeContext` on the host beside `themeContext`, and `applyLocale` assigns a new language into that shallow observable, never through `provider.set()`, which a component mounted after a switch would miss; nothing in i18n is frozen, since the element assigns into it. `div.root` carries `lang` and `dir`, and nothing is set on the host element or the page. A component takes `useI18n(ctx)` at setup and reads `i18n.value.t` inside its render function; code outside a component that hands a template on (a toast) writes `localized(key, params)` (`components/localized/Localized.tsx`), read where it renders, so it follows a switch too; a function that needs a string now takes `i18n: I18n = sourceI18n` as its last parameter, its caller passing the live one. Where nothing provides one, `localeContext` reads `sourceI18n`, English, as in a spec that mounts a component alone.
- **`locale` names the display language** everywhere (`LocaleOption`, `setLocale`, `changeLocale`, `enableLocalePicker`), apart from `settings.language`, the `Language` enum and `LockSettingType.language`, which are the code generator's. No prop is named `lang` or `dir`, which `HTMLElement` already has.
- **Dictionaries** (`i18n/messages/`): `en/` is the source, one file per part of the editor, each key flat and prefixed with its file's name (`common.close`, `findReplace.matchCount`), spread into one `as const` `en` (`en/en.test.ts` holds that shape); `common` holds the words several parts share. Every other language is a `<code>.ts` exporting its code without the hyphen (`koKR`) `satisfies Messages`, and `messages.test.ts` holds each to English key for key and shape for shape: the same placeholders, a plural's forms exactly its language's `Intl.PluralRules` categories with `{count}` in each, `ERD Editor`, `ERD`, `SQL`, `PNG`, `SVG` and `JSON` kept wherever English writes them, no bidi control, no empty or padded text, and a `common.placeholder.*` of 12 code points at most. Text the editor gives a fixed width fits it in every language: a `common.placeholder.*` measures, as `utils/text.ts` measures a value, no wider than an empty column (`COLUMN_MIN_WIDTH`), so a shorter standard word or a standard abbreviation stands in for a long one (`по умолч.`, `obs.`), or the SQL keyword where a language has neither (Hebrew's `DEFAULT`), `toolbar.databaseName` fits the toolbar's input (`TOOLBAR_DATABASE_NAME_WIDTH`), and the three labels of each Schema SQL options segment group, Statements (the literals `Create`, `If not exists` and `Drop & re-create`) and Header (`common.none` beside the literal `USE` and `CREATE + USE`), fit 207 px of the 300 px panel (`SCHEMA_SQL_PANEL_WIDTH`). `messages/fit.browser.test.ts` measures all three, every `common.placeholder.*` key picked by prefix, in the font a Mac resolves the stack to, and runs on a Mac only (`it.runIf` on `server.platform`, the Vitest server's `process.platform`): the bounds were fitted to that font, and other platforms resolve the stack to fonts wider in places, the Playwright Linux image cutting ko-KR's `데이터 타입` and DejaVu Sans eight placeholders and pt-PT's database name (measured 2026-10-06, when the bounds were set), so elsewhere `messages.test.ts`'s 12-code-point cap is the check. `messages/index.ts` imports every dictionary statically, never with `import()`: Obsidian loads one `main.js`, the UMD build is one file, and `pnpm size` counts a dynamic chunk too. Only the element and the specs import its `messagesOf` (graphs below).
- **Keys and parameters.** A message interpolates `{name}` alone, with no ICU syntax, a number written as `String(n)`. A plural, an object of forms that `t()` picks among by the language's `Intl.PluralRules` (`other` where a form is missing), exists only where English shows the number (`findReplace.matchCount`, `palette.showMatches` and `colorPicker.degrees` among them); a choice English makes without one is two plain keys picked in code (`contextMenu.deleteColumn` / `deleteColumns`), since a category such as Russian's `one` also covers 21. A sentence is one message with its parts as placeholders, never fragments joined in code, and a word no language translates enters as one (`{format}`, `{database}`). Every label map and every `labelKey`, `titleKey` or `nameKey` is typed `PlainMessageKey` (`menuLabel`, `LnbLabelKey`, `SCOPE_LABEL`, `ColumnTypeToMessageKey`, which `components/settings/columnTypeLabels.ts` keeps out of `constants/schema.ts`, a module the peer graph reaches), since `t()` handed the whole `MessageKey` union asks for a parameters argument. Under a right-to-left language `t()` wraps each value in U+2068 and U+2069, so a name or a number keeps its own direction, and English output stays byte for byte what it was; the source writes the two as `\u2068` / `\u2069` escapes, never as characters. A list of names joined in code isolates each name the same way (`isolate`, as the Schema SQL drop warning does), so its conjunction, not a Latin name, sets the direction the list runs in.
- **What stays as written** is a literal in code, never a key: database vendors, code languages, file formats, SQL keywords and referential actions, the Schema SQL statements and headers (`Create`, `If not exists`, `Drop & re-create`, `USE`, `CREATE + USE`, an owner decision of 2026-10-07), data types, `PK`, `UQ`, `AI`, `N-N`, `NULL`, `AK<n>.<m>`, `D:C U:R`, the name cases and quote styles, key names, Hex, R, G and B, `{n}x`, `px` and `%`, the Radix color keys the theme builder names its swatches by, generated output and exported file names, and developer errors. Each language names itself in the picker (`LOCALES`' `label`), its English name only a hidden search term of the palette. The tab is Code Generator wherever it is named (`common.tab.codeGenerator`), the palette's row included.
- **The canvas stays left to right.** Each root that owns an on-screen stage (`Canvas.tsx`, which the Flow view, the diff viewer, time travel and the placement preview reuse, `Minimap.tsx`, `VisualizationGraph.tsx`) carries `dir="ltr"`, which konva's `Text` reads through its canvas, and the export stage is detached, so it resolves to `ltr`. The parts that read left to right in any language carry it on their roots too: the floating and Visualization toolbars, the content compass, the scrollbars, the column data type cell and Map Columns' data types, the code block, the Schema SQL and Code Generator tabs' rows of code and options panel (`SchemaSQL.tsx`, `GeneratorCode.tsx`; each panel takes the reader's direction back on its own root) and the Schema SQL panel's script editors (`ScriptEditor.tsx`), the color picker, the diff viewer, time travel and its slider, the automatic placement preview, `Kbd`, `Slider`, `Switch`, the welcome screen's arrows, and a menu row's name kept as written (`Menu`'s `literal`: the vendors, the code languages and the referential actions, so `C#` never reads `#C`). `Kbd` is `width: fit-content`, so in a right-to-left cell it starts where the cell does. It is always the attribute on a root, never a stylesheet rule: a global `.konvajs-content` rule would move the global bucket the `styles/emittedCss.*.test.ts` specs pin. Other physical CSS became logical (`margin-inline-*`, `inset-inline-*`, `border-inline-*`), except Find and Replace, which keeps the canvas's left in every language (`left: 16px`), since `coveredWidth` measures from that edge (`PANEL_LEFT`) of a canvas that never mirrors, the draw-target layer, whose outline, strip and buttons are placed by `left` in the canvas's coordinates, so the buttons stand on a table's left in every language (`DrawTargetButtons.browser.test.tsx` checks it in Arabic), the table group draw's box (`TableGroupDraft.tsx`), placed by `left` and a translate in the erd root's pixels, which `useTableGroupDraw` measures from the root's left edge, so the box spans from the press in every language (`tableGroupCommands.browser.test.tsx` checks it right to left), and the options panels' `border-left` (the Code Generator panel's is the Schema SQL panel's `shell.panel`), its rule beside the code in every language, since the row is `ltr` while the panel follows the reader (`SchemaSQLOptions.browser.test.tsx` checks it in Arabic). A context menu opens at the pointer in every language, and a submenu opens to the left of its row under a right-to-left language (`ContextMenuItem` passes `openLeft`, which `fitMenu` flips only when the left edge cuts it more), its chevron (`SubmenuChevron`) turned to match; the toolbar's undo and redo swap glyphs, so undo points back the way a right-to-left reader goes. Text that may run either way carries `dir="auto"`: the palette's names and keywords, Find and Replace's rows, Table Properties' chips, Map Columns' column names, the toolbar's database name, the Settings lock values, and the palette's input and Find and Replace's two inputs while they hold text (`TextInput`'s `dir`; an empty one follows the page, since Chromium sets an empty auto field left to right); a Find and Replace row's text and a palette row's keywords align to the panel's side, `left`, or `right` under `[dir='rtl']` (Chromium ignores `match-parent`), so text in the other direction still sits by its icon or its rule.
- **The export worker draws in the language it is handed.** `ExportImage` snapshots `{ locale, messages }` (`LocaleMessages`) as it opens and hands it with every preview, PNG and SVG request; `documentScene` builds `createI18n` from it and `renderScene` provides `localeContext`, so a file draws its placeholders in the language the dialog opened in, and a request without it draws English (`realm.test.ts`, `renderSvg.browser.test.ts`).
- **Peer and worker graphs.** `i18n/imports.test.ts` keeps every `i18n/` module, `components/localeContext.ts` and `components/localized/` out of what `peer/index.ts` and `engine/index.ts` reach, and lets the four SharedWorkers reach only `translate`, `locales`, `source`, `menuLabel` and `messages/en/**` of `i18n/`, never `messages/index.ts` or another language, since the export worker is handed its dictionary. A module either graph reaches imports an i18n type with `import type { … }` (`utils/emitter.ts`'s `LocaleOption`): the walker the graph specs share (`__test-utils__/importGraph.ts`) counts `import { type … }` as a value import.
- **The palette's Theme and Display Language** (`createPreferenceActions`, `quick-search/actions.tsx`; owner decisions of 2026-10-06) show on every tab, each only while `enableThemeBuilder` or `enableLocalePicker` is on: Theme lists System, Light and Dark and sends `setThemeOptionsAction` with the appearance alone, so the gray and accent stay; Display Language lists System, then each language in its own name; each checks the option in force. A translated row keeps the English of its name and keywords in `Action.alias` (`named`), which a search reads, Fuse included, and the list never shows, so a command is found by its English words too; a language row's alias is its English name and its code, and a table row ignores the field. A submenu opens with the keyboard on its option in force (`Action.checked`), scrolled to it, as the locale picker opens. An open palette builds its level again when the language or an option in force changes (`rebuild` in `QuickSearch.tsx`), keeping what is typed and the submenu shown, found again by its row's message key (`Action.id`), or the top level once the host took that row away, so no row keeps the language it opened in.
- **The locale picker** (`components/locale-picker/`, mounted by `ErdEditor` while `enableLocalePicker` is on) toggles from the toolbar's language button (`.toolbar-locale`) through `openLocalePickerAction`. It lists System, with the language it stands for now beside it, then the 25 languages, each label carrying its own `lang`; System and the rule under it stay pinned at the list's top (`position: sticky`, with the list's `scroll-padding-block-start` keeping a row clear of them); it opens focused on the checked row, scrolled to it, the arrows go round, Home and End jump, Enter or Space picks, and every bound chord but `PANEL_PASSING_BINDINGS` stops at it, as in Find and Replace. A pick emits `setLocaleOptionAction`, closes it and hands the keyboard back with `focusEvent`. Opening it closes the theme builder and Table Properties; opening the theme builder, Find and Replace or the palette closes it, as do `stop` and a press outside it and the toolbar (`handlePress`).
- **`components/settings/settingsPage.ts` opens a Settings page from outside the tab**, the welcome screen's Shortcuts row: `requestSettingsPage(store, page)` records the page for that store and switches to the Settings tab, which takes it once as it mounts (`takeSettingsPage`); reached any other way, the tab opens on Preferences.
- **Specs read English.** happy-dom and Playwright Test run in `en-US`, and `vitest.config.ts` pins the browser provider to `contextOptions: { locale: 'en-US' }`, since an element following System shows the browser's language. The e2e fixture keeps both props off; a spec turns them on with `ErdEditorPage.enable(…)` and gives System its language with `test.use({ locale })`. `__test-utils__/i18n.ts`'s `pseudoMessages(tag)` (`xx:Close`), `createTestI18n` and `provideI18n` tell a string read through `t()` from a literal left behind, and switching the language under a mounted component catches a string read at setup instead of in the render.

**Welcome screen**

- **It stands over an empty diagram** (`components/erd/welcome-screen/`, owner decisions of 2026-10-06). `Erd` mounts it, between the minimap and the floating toolbar, while `enableWelcomeScreen` is on, the editor is not readonly, not in zen mode, not taken over and not showing Table Properties, the element is not yet destroyed (`AppContext.lifecycle.destroyed`, which `appDestroy` sets first, so the clear that `destroy()` dispatches raises no screen over the providers it removed), and the document holds no table, no memo and no shown group (`isEmptyDocument`, the content bounds' own `hasContent`, so the map and the scrollbars come as it leaves), so the first table, memo or shown group takes it away and an undo back to none brings it again; `Erd` itself is the ERD tab's alone. It shows a heading, with no logo and no product name, and a menu: New Table, New Memo, Import, which swaps in its formats (`createImportMenus`) behind a Back row that Escape presses too, Command Palette and Shortcuts (`requestSettingsPage`). Where the canvas has room, hand-drawn arrows point at the toolbar's Search button, at its theme and language buttons and at the floating toolbar. `welcomeTiers` reads the canvas less what Find and Replace covers (`coveredWidth`): nothing under 320 px wide or 294 px tall, the menu alone under 342 px tall, the arrows from 720 by 562 px, a row's chord from 480 px wide; the arrows also leave while Find and Replace, the theme builder or the locale picker is up. The three heights are what the block needs, measured by `WelcomeScreen.browser.test.ts`: Import's six rows alone fill the room over the floating toolbar at 294 on every platform, since a row keeps its height in any font, and, in every language on a Mac, a heading of two lines over them at 320 px wide fills it at 342, and at 562 the arrows clear that block with nothing to spare in the language whose labels wrap the most.
- **Only its menu takes the pointer.** The layer is `pointer-events: none` and the menu `auto`, so a drag over the heading pans, while `.welcome-screen-menu` joins the `canDrag` list in `Erd.tsx`, so a press on a row starts none. The arrows find their buttons by the toolbar's anchor classes, `.toolbar-search`, `.toolbar-theme` and `.toolbar-locale` (`measureAnchors`, the theme and language buttons sharing the point between them), measured again as the viewport, the tab, the props or the direction changes, and they mirror with the toolbar under a right-to-left language. Its paint never comes from a stylesheet `fill` (`emittedCss.iconPaint.test.ts`): the arrows (`welcome-hints/hintArrows.tsx`) take `fill="none"` and `stroke="currentColor"` attributes.
- **Its rows keep the editor's keys.** ArrowUp and ArrowDown, Home and End walk the rows round, Enter and Space press the row, and every other chord reaches the editor, so `Alt+N` still adds a table. A row that takes the menu away (New Table, New Memo, Shortcuts) hands the keyboard back with `focusEvent`, and so does the screen when it leaves while a row holds focus, as when an import or a chord pressed on a row brings the first table; without it the keyboard would fall to the page.

**Peer store (`peer.js`)**

- **The peer graph runs in Node with no DOM and no bundler of its own**: `mcp-server` inlines what `src/peer/index.ts` reaches into one file, so that graph obeys the two rules root `AGENTS.md` gives under `pnpm peer-graph`, no browser global and no package outside its allowlist besides the workspace libraries it inlines. `src/peer/imports.test.ts` holds the sources to them in `pnpm test` and `pnpm peer-graph` holds `dist/` after `pnpm build`; their allowlists change together. A shared helper that starts reaching konva, shiki, elkjs, comlink or a worker spawn is how that breaks.
- **The entry carries values, never tools.** `src/peer/index.test.ts` pins its key set and the absence of the flat `actions` map, whose `changeZIndexAction` would come from whichever module spread last. Tool names, the registry, argument validation, reading and the reachability lists belong to `mcp-server`; `engine/no-agent-concepts.test.ts` fails on any of them reappearing in `src/`.
- **Eight keys are there for specs alone** — `createEngineContext`, `ChangeActionTypes`, `StreamActionTypes`, `SharedFollowingActionTypes`, `SelectType`, `settingsActions$`, `CanvasType` (a tab the snapshot and fingerprint specs move to) and `SaveSettingType` (the switch a new document carries for releases before the locks, `create.test.ts`); `defaultToWidth` serves those specs and also sizes tables for `erd_list` (`mcp-server`'s `src/tools/outline.ts`). `mcp-server` holds each tool declaration to what the engine measurably does, so its specs need the real context, the real text measure, the real type lists and the real generator barrel; a `./peer/testing.js` subpath would make a fourth entry and split the chunks again. `mcp-server`'s `src/peerSurface.test.ts` proves no key is dead. `app`'s Drive and fingerprint specs import the entry too (`createPeerStore`, the table, column and settings barrels, `CanvasType`, `LockSettingType`, and `SharedFollowingActionTypes`, which `documentController.test.ts` holds `NON_EDIT_ACTIONS` to), so a change to the key set runs `app`'s `test` beside `mcp-server`'s.
- **The labelled undo unit is the peer's own bookkeeping**, not the engine's: `dispatch(actions, { label })` records one unit, `group(label, run)` gathers the dispatches `run` makes into one unit under its own label (a group inside a group joins the outer one, a group that dispatched nothing leaves none, and `undo`, `redo` and `setInitialValue` throw inside one, since the unit would no longer match the history), `undo` pops the newest that made an entry and names the labels it passed over, and `setInitialValue` forgets all of them. The engine's history knows only entries.
- **Stream buffer option.** `createRxStore(context, { manualStreamFlush })` and `createSharedStore(store, config, { manualStreamFlush })` close a stream group (colors, memo resizes) on `flushStreamBuffers()` instead of `groupByStreamActions`' 200 ms quiet period, through `flushOnNotifier` over a `Subject` the store owns. Each store owns its tick count too: one in the rx-store's single history stage; `FLUSH_TICKS = 2` in the shared store, whose compressor stands on each side of the circuit breaker and whose second stage arms too late for the first tick. So `stream-flush.ts` flushes history first, then the outbound pipe, and knows neither the count nor an rxjs type. Without the flag the rx-store's `flushStreamBuffers` is a no-op and the editor keeps its timing: a stream edit reaches peers after the two stages' 200 ms each, 400 ms. The shared store's still works there (`quietPeriodOrNotifier`, the quiet period or the flush, whichever comes first), so a host can send a drag still held back before it lets the store go; `obsidian-plugin` does when a tab closes, so the other tabs of its file keep what it held. The shared store takes it as an internal third argument (`SharedStoreInternalOptions`), so neither `SharedStoreConfig` nor the config `getSharedStore` takes carries a stream option.
- `flushOnNotifier` and `createSharedStreamActionsCompressor` are deep-imported and stay out of the `rx-operators` barrel: `rx-operators/index.test.ts` pins the barrel's export set, as it keeps `readonlyIgnoreFilter` out.
- **`createRxStore`'s internal `observable` option** (default true) keeps the state a plain object. r-html's reactive proxy reads `value instanceof Node`, a `ReferenceError` in plain Node; the replica store never hit it because it already ran with observation off. The peer passes false and first dispatches `changeViewportAction({ width: 0, height: 0 })`, so a load never reaches the pull's view lookup. `RxStoreOptions` is not public surface.
- **A replicated sort sends `table.moveTo`, never `table.sort`.** `sortTable` places by each replica's own `toWidth`, so replaying it lands tables apart; `sortTablesToMoveAction$` runs the reducer on copies of the live tables and groups and sends the absolute points, and each group with members its `tableGroup.resize`, in one undoable batch. An import's `table.sort` is safe because the widths travel inside its `loadJson` payload.

**Clipboard, packaging, dependencies**

- **Duplicates** (`utils/table-clipboard/copy.ts`, `engine/modules/editor/utils/duplicate.ts`): a relationship rides only when both ends are copied, an index whole or not at all, `index.add` before its `indexColumn.add`s — one batch of atom actions, never `attachChangeOnlyTag$` or a second dispatch, so one undo reverts it. `CLIPBOARD_VERSION` stays 1 (a bump makes shipped editors refuse new copies); `PayloadKind.columns` carries neither array.
- **No `sideEffects` field, no `preserveModules`.** Vite reads the package's own field against `src/`, so a `./dist/…` list marks `customElementRegistry.ts` pure and drops element registration. A future list needs `**/` globs covering the entry, `customElementRegistry`, `ErdEditor`, `GlobalStyles`, `konva/batchDraw`, `konva/host`, `konva/workerDomStubs` and `services/elk-layout/elkWorkerRealm`.
- A third-party import in shipped `src/` goes in `dependencies`: the lib and worker builds externalize exactly that list, so a `devDependencies` entry inlines and its types stop resolving for consumers. The private `@dineug/*` libraries inline, though the public `.d.ts` still name `@dineug/r-html` and `@dineug/erd-editor-schema`.
- `@/*` → `src/*` is declared in `tsconfig.json`, `e2e/tsconfig.json`, `vite.config.ts`, `vitest.config.ts` and `vite.umd.config.ts` (after the `@/workers/spawn` entry; aliases match in order). A new alias needs all five.

### Testing Requirements

- `pnpm exec vp run --filter @dineug/erd-editor --fail-if-no-match test` — `tsc --noEmit`, then `unit` (happy-dom; `vitest.setup.ts` stubs observers, `matchMedia`, `requestIdleCallback` and a 2D context that draws nothing) and `browser` (headless Chromium). **The `.browser.` infix alone picks the project**; a Konva spec without it runs on the stub context and fails for unrelated reasons.
- `pnpm --filter @dineug/erd-editor test:coverage` / `test:dev` — built-in `vp test`, no type gate. CI runs `test:coverage` over both projects at `perFile` 80%. The three `if (import.meta.hot)` blocks (`Canvas.tsx`, `Minimap.tsx`, `VisualizationGraph.tsx`) carry `/* v8 ignore next -- @preserve */`, since no test runner defines `import.meta.hot`; `@preserve` keeps the hint through the TypeScript transform. Any other gap takes a test. For one file, pass `--project unit` or `--project browser` with `--coverage.include`: without a project the filter matches nothing.
- `pnpm --filter @dineug/erd-editor dev` builds the workspace dependencies, then serves `vp dev` (`index.html` → `src/index.dev.ts`, r-html HMR); `dev:storybook` / `build:storybook` run Storybook 10.
- `pnpm --filter @dineug/erd-editor e2e` (builds first); also `e2e:dev`, `e2e:headed`, `e2e:report`, `e2e:typecheck`, `e2e:bench`. Read `e2e/README.md` first: the fixture reopens the closed shadow root, and `E2E_PORT` picks the port.
- A DOM spec stays `.ts` with tagged templates (proving JSX changed nothing); a scene spec is `.tsx` under the konva pragma. `.vitest/` (`.vitest-attachments/` before Vitest 5) and `__screenshots__/` are gitignored and never review evidence.
- A view spec provides the source on its container **before** rendering: `useContext` subscribes at setup and `onBeforeMount` only.
- Everything mounting `canvas/Canvas.tsx` registers as `canvas`; `konva/testHandle.ts` publishes the newest live claim, so `__erdStages.canvas` is the surface on top.
- The export-png worker builds its own app context, so scene code counts twice in `pnpm size`, and so do `components/localeContext.ts`, `i18n/{source,translate,locales}.ts` and the English dictionary (`i18n/messages/en/**`), which the scene's placeholders read; only removing a live reference moves bytes. Report a number rather than re-pinning the budget.
- `src/peer/` specs, and in `src/engine/` those of `peer-store.*`, `presence`, `stream-flush` and `to-width`, open with `// @vitest-environment node`, since the peer's real realm has no DOM; `engine/peer-store.node.test.ts` runs one with `window`, `document` and `Node` undefined. `engine/peer-store.converge.test.ts` wires a peer store and a user store the way `e2e/specs/shared-presence.spec.ts` wires two elements, runs every `SEED_SCENARIOS` edit through them (its mapping races wire two to five peer stores through `__test-utils__/peerMesh.ts`, the hooks run on fake timers after every batch delivered), and is one of the three completion gates of the peer work with `mcp-server`'s `scenarios.test.ts` and `vuerd-vscode`'s `agent-hub.test.ts`.
- `engine/peer-store.undoable.test.ts` and `peer-store.dispatch-count.test.ts` are the two measurements only this package can make: the first replays each `SEED_SCENARIOS` edit through a bare `createRxStore` and compares the cursor it moves with the entries `dispatch` reported, and the second mocks `@/engine/rx-store` to count the calls into `dispatchSync`. Run against `mcp-server`'s own peer they would check a report against itself.
- `dist/peer/index.js` (about 3 kB gzip) and the `shared-store` chunk it shares with the element count in `pnpm size` though no editor loads the entry; the budget note says what the measurement was when it was re-pinned.

### Common Patterns

- One directory per component: `Foo.tsx` + `Foo.styles.ts` + `Foo.test.ts` (+ `Foo.stories.tsx`), mounted via `src/__test-utils__` (`mountAndFlush`, `flush`). A scene component has no `.styles.ts` (colours from `@/konva/theme`, geometry from `konva/scene/metrics.ts`, `canvas/sceneTokens.ts`) and its spec is `Foo.browser.test.tsx`.
- **JSX → tagged templates** (`@dineug/vite-plugin-r-html`): `class` / `style` keep array and object values; `?x` → `bool:x`, `@x` → `on:x`, `.x` → `prop:x` on a DOM element; `${ref(r)}` → `use:ref={ref(r)}`; `...${o}` → `{...o}`. Component props take no dot — the transform adds it, so `onFoo` is never an event. `<C>{t}</C>` compiles to `` .children=${html`${t}`} ``.
- A second binding of one event is `on:x__2`; an empty template has no JSX spelling, so `GlobalStyles.ts` and `Icon.tsx` keep tagged ones. `src/__jsx-parity__/parity.test.tsx` compares both spellings' DOM.
- **Konva files** open with `/** @jsxHost konva */` and compile against `konvaImportSource: '@/konva/host'` (all three Vite configs). Intrinsics: `k-circle`, `k-group`, `k-layer`, `k-line`, `k-path`, `k-rect`, `k-text` (`src/konva/jsx.d.ts`). Mixing hosts, `class` / `style` / `zIndex`, a spread or a text child fail the compile; `bool:`, `prop:`, `draggable`, `on:drag*` are typed `never`. `parity.konva.browser.test.tsx` gates it.
- Colour an icon with `color`, never `fill` (`emittedCss.iconPaint.test.ts`); the scene draws the same `getIcon` data via `canvas/SceneIcon.template.tsx`.
- **A native list goes in `primitives/select/Select.tsx`**, which the Schema SQL and Code Generator panels and Map Columns use, since the browser's own arrow sat against the list's end, nearer it than the value stood to the start (the owner's report on 2026-10-10): the caller writes the `<select>`, its options, name and change as the children and sets only the box's width; the box turns the browser's arrow off and lays lucide's `chevron-down` (14 px, coloured by `color`) over the list's inline end, its ink 8.9 px past the border, where a value's first letter stands past its 8 px padding on the other side, the value ellipsized 26 px short of that end, a press passing through to the list, on the left under a right-to-left reader, dimmed with the value by `dimmed` or a disabled list and `FieldText` in forced colours (`Select.browser.test.ts`). Its focus ring stands 1 px off, as the panels ring their other controls; Map Columns rings its lists 2 px off, as it rings its buttons, a doubled class in `MapColumnsDialog.styles.ts` outweighing the box's rule (`MapColumnsDialog.browser.test.tsx`).
- A table is drawn with lucide's `table-2` wherever one shows (an owner decision): the table headers on the canvas and in the Visualization tab, New Table in the context menu, the palette and the welcome screen, the diff tree, the palette's table rows and a table name found (`find-replace/fieldIcon.tsx`, which the palette's `#` rows share); a table or column comment found draws `message-square`. The Find and Replace command draws `text-search` wherever it shows, the toolbar button, the context menu and the palette row (an owner decision), leaving `replace` to the panel's own Replace button. `icons.test.ts` fails on a registered icon no module names, so a glyph that goes out of use leaves `icons.ts`, and the bundle, with it; `__test-utils__/icon.ts`'s `iconNameOf` tells a spec which glyph an element draws.
- `somethingAction$` generators live in `generator.actions.ts`, `somethingAction` creators in `atom.actions.ts`; bitmask flags go through `src/utils/bit.ts`. `VisualizationToolbar` reuses `FloatingToolbar.styles.ts`, so restyle both bars there.
- `constants/sql/dataType/` lists each vendor's types with their `primitiveType`, the column cell's data type hints and the category the code generators fall back to: `generator-code/columnTypes.ts` reads a type by name first (Code generators, below), so a type a list adds reaches the generators through its category unless the classifier or a generator has a rule for its name; `schema-sql-parser`'s `src/parser/dataType/` keeps a list per vendor, which its `helper.ts` merges into the one set its parser matches type names against, word by word, and no test pins the two, so a type added or renamed in one goes into the other (`packages/schema-sql-parser/AGENTS.md`).
- **Code generators — deliberate, not bugs:**
  - **One vendor type classifier** (`columnTypes.ts`, an owner decision of 2026-10-09). `getColumnType(dataType, database)` reads a column's type by name before the shared vendor tables, which file each name under one of eleven categories, and answers what the generators need: the scalar (`bool`, integers by width and sign, `f32` or `f64`, `decimal`, `string`, `bytes`, `uuid`, `json`, `date`, `time`, `timeTz`, `dateTime`, `dateTimeUtc` for an instant shown in the session's zone, `dateTimeOffset`, `interval`); the base name, lower case with its arguments gone and, on MySQL and MariaDB, its UNSIGNED, ZEROFILL and SIGNED too; the numeric arguments; the PostgreSQL array depth (`splitPostgresArray`); an integer's width (24 for MEDIUMINT); a character type's declared length; a decimal's precision and scale (`NUMBER(*,s)` precision 38); an interval's fields (year to month, day to second, or both); the network kind; an ENUM's or SET's members (`enumMembers`, each read as MySQL reads a string literal); and whether the type is unsigned (UNSIGNED, ZEROFILL or SERIAL on MySQL and MariaDB), a serial, a float, money, SQL Server's `uniqueidentifier` (`isGuid`: any 16 bytes, not only an RFC UUID) or `rowversion`, semi-structured (Snowflake's and Databricks' VARIANT, OBJECT, ARRAY, MAP, STRUCT), a PostgreSQL bit string, PostgreSQL's `xid`, `cid` or `xid8` (`isTextInteger`: node-postgres hands them over as text), Databricks' `VOID` (`isNullOnly`) and named by the database's vendor list (`isListed`). `columnScalar` gives the width a name declares, and `getColumnType` widens every integer of Oracle (a NUMBER(38)), Snowflake (a NUMBER(38,0)) and SQLite (eight bytes) to `i64`.
